.

Next, let us prove that

the fixed point set of the induced
S^{1}
-action on

({\bf H}_{E_{\gamma_{\mu}}})_{\mu}^{0}:={\rm Ker}(\check{\mathscr{L}}^{\prime\prime}_{\mu}(0))
is
\{0\}
,

(2.15)

Note that
\xi\in{\bf H}_{E_{\gamma_{\mu}}}
belongs to

({\bf H}_{E_{\gamma_{\mu}}})_{\mu}^{0}
if and only if it is
C^{2}
and satisfies

\partial_{xy}\check{L}_{\lambda}(0,0)\dot{\xi}+\partial_{yy}\check{L}_{\lambda}(0,0)\ddot{\xi}-\partial_{xx}\check{L}_{\lambda}(0,0)\xi-\partial_{yx}\check{L}_{\lambda}(0,0)\dot{\xi}=0.

(2.16)

Suppose that
\xi
is also a fixed point for the action in (
2.6
).
Then it is equal to a constant vector in
\mathbb{R}^{n}

and
E_{\gamma_{\mu}}\xi=\xi
. By (
2.16
) we obtain

\partial_{xx}\check{L}_{\lambda}(0,0)\xi=0
and hence
\xi=0
because

\partial_{xx}\check{L}_{\mu}(0,0)
is positive definite by (L0). (
2.15
) is proved.

By
[
25
, Theorem C.8]
(
[
24
, Theorem 3.7]
or
[
22
, Theorem 5.12]
) and
[
24
, Remark 3.9]
(or
[
22
, Remark 5.14]
)
one of the following alternatives occurs:

(I)

(\mu,0)
is not an isolated solution in
\{\mu\}\times\mathcal{U}_{E_{\gamma_{\mu}}}

of
\nabla\check{\mathscr{L}}_{\mu}=0
.

(II)

There exist left and right neighborhoods
\Lambda^{-}
and
\Lambda^{+}
of
\mu
in
\Lambda

and integers
n^{+},n^{-}\geq 0
, such that
n^{+}+n^{-}\geq\frac{1}{2}\dim({\bf H}_{E_{\gamma_{\mu}}})_{\mu}^{0}
,
and that for
\lambda\in\Lambda^{-}\setminus\{\mu\}
(resp.
\lambda\in\Lambda^{+}\setminus\{\mu\}
)
the functional
\check{\mathscr{L}}_{\lambda}
has at least
n^{-}
(resp.
n^{+}
) distinct critical

S^{1}
-orbits disjoint with
0
, which converge to
0
in
\mathcal{U}^{X}_{E_{\gamma_{\mu}}}
as
\lambda\to\mu
.

Moreover, if
\dim({\bf H}_{E_{\gamma_{\mu}}})_{\mu}^{0}\geq 3
, then

[
25
, Theorem C.9]
(or
[
24
, Theorem 3.10]
)
shows that (II) may be replaced by the following alternatives:

(III)

For every
\lambda\in\Lambda\setminus\{0\}
near
0\in\Lambda
there is a

S^{1}
-orbit
S^{1}\cdot\bar{w}_{\lambda}\neq\{0\}
near
0\in\mathcal{U}^{X}_{E_{\gamma_{\mu}}}

such that
\nabla\check{\mathscr{L}}_{\lambda}(\bar{w}_{\lambda})=0
and that

S^{1}\cdot\bar{w}_{\lambda}\to 0
in
\mathcal{U}^{X}_{E_{\gamma_{\mu}}}
as
\lambda\to\mu
.

(IV)

For any small
S^{1}
-invariant neighborhood
\mathcal{N}
of
0
in
\mathcal{U}^{X}_{E_{\gamma_{\mu}}}

there is a one-sided deleted neighborhood
\Lambda^{0}
of
\mu\in\Lambda
such that
for any
\lambda\in\Lambda^{0}
,
\nabla\check{\mathscr{L}}_{\lambda}=0

has either infinitely many
S^{1}
-orbits of solutions in
\mathcal{N}
,

S^{1}\cdot\bar{w}^{j}_{\lambda}
,
j=1,2,\cdots
,
or at least two
S^{1}
-orbits of solutions in
\mathcal{N}
,

S^{1}\cdot\hat{w}^{1}_{\lambda}\neq\{0\}
and
S^{1}\cdot\hat{w}^{2}_{\lambda}\neq\{0\}
, such that
\check{\mathscr{L}}_{\lambda}(\hat{w}^{1}_{\lambda})\neq\check{\mathscr{L}}_{\lambda}(\hat{w}^{2}_{\lambda})
.
Moreover, these orbits converge to
0
in
\mathcal{U}^{X}_{E_{\gamma_{\mu}}}
as
\lambda\to\mu
.

Step 3
(
Complete the proof of Theorem
1.4
).

In the case of (I)
, we have a sequence
(w_{j})\subset{\bf H}_{E_{\gamma_{\mu}}}\setminus\{0\}

such that
\|w_{j}\|_{1,2}\to 0
as
j\to\infty
and that
\nabla\check{\mathscr{L}}_{\mu}(w_{j})=0
for each
j\in{\mathbb{N}}
.
By Proposition
2.3
these
w_{j}
are
C^{2}
and
\|w_{j}\|_{C^{2}}\to 0
.
Because
0
is a fixed point for the
S^{1}
-action, the
S^{1}
-orbits are compact and different
S^{1}
-orbits
are not intersecting, by passing to a subsequence we can assume that any two of
w_{j}
,
j=0,1,\cdots
,
do not belong the same
S^{1}
-orbit. Using the chart
\phi
in (
2.1
)
we define
\mathbb{R}\ni t\mapsto\gamma^{k}(t):=\phi(w_{k}(t))
,
k=1,\cdots
. They satisfy (i) of Theorem
1.4
.

In the case of (II)
, note firstly that
\dim({\bf H}_{E_{\gamma_{\mu}}})_{\mu}^{0}
is equal to

m^{0}_{\tau}(\check{\mathscr{L}}_{\mu},0)=m^{0}_{\tau}(\mathfrak{E}_{\mu},\gamma_{\mu})
.
For
\lambda\in\Lambda^{-}\setminus\{\mu\}
(resp.
\lambda\in\Lambda^{+}\setminus\{\mu\}
) let

S^{1}\cdot w_{\lambda}^{i}
,
i=1,\cdots,n^{-}
(resp.
n^{+}
)
be distinct critical
S^{1}
-orbits of
\check{\mathscr{L}}_{\lambda}

disjoint with
0
, which converge to
0
in
\mathcal{U}^{X}_{E_{\gamma_{\mu}}}
as
\lambda\to\mu
.
Proposition
2.3
implies that
\|w_{\lambda}^{i}\|_{C^{2}}\to 0
as
\lambda\to\mu
.
Then
\mathbb{R}\ni t\mapsto\gamma_{\lambda}^{i}(t)=\phi(x_{\lambda}(t)+w_{\lambda}^{i}(t))
,
i=1,\cdots,n^{-}
(resp.
n^{+}
) are the required solutions.

In the case of (III)
, let
\alpha_{\lambda}(t)=\phi(x_{\lambda}(t)+\bar{w}_{\lambda}(t))
for
t\in\mathbb{R}
.
It satisfies (
1.4
) with parameter value
\lambda
and Proposition
2.3

implies that
\alpha_{\lambda}-\gamma_{\lambda}
converges to zero
on any compact interval
I\subset{\mathbb{R}}
in
C^{2}
-topology as
\lambda\to\mu
.
Since
S^{1}\cdot\bar{w}_{\lambda}\neq\{0\}
, for any
t\in\mathbb{R}
we have
\bar{w}_{\lambda}(t)\neq 0
and so
\alpha_{\lambda}(t)\neq\gamma_{\lambda}(t)
.
Note that all
\gamma_{\lambda}
are constant solutions. Hence
\alpha_{\lambda}\notin{\mathbb{R}}\cdot\gamma_{\lambda}
.

In the case of (IV)
, for the first case
let
\alpha^{j}_{\lambda}(t)=\phi(x_{\lambda}(t)+\bar{w}^{j}_{\lambda}(t))
for
t\in\mathbb{R}
and
j=1,2,\cdots
,
and for the second case let
\beta^{i}_{\lambda}(t)=\phi(x_{\lambda}(t)+\hat{w}^{i}_{\lambda}(t))
for
t\in\mathbb{R}
and
i=1,2
.
They satisfy (
1.4
) with parameter value
\lambda
.
For a given small
\epsilon>0
, by Proposition
2.3

there is an one-sided neighborhood
\Lambda^{0}
of
\mu
in
\Lambda
such that
for any
\lambda\in\Lambda^{0}\setminus\{\mu\}
,

\bullet

for the first case
\|\bar{\alpha}_{\lambda}^{k}|_{[0,\tau]}-\gamma_{\lambda}|_{[0,\tau]}\|_{C^{2}([0,\tau];\mathbb{R}^{N})}<\epsilon
,
k=1,2,\cdots
,

\bullet

for the second case
\|{\beta}_{\lambda}^{i}|_{[0,\tau]}-\gamma_{\lambda}|_{[0,\tau]}\|_{C^{2}([0,\tau];\mathbb{R}^{N})}<\epsilon
,
i=1,2
.

Moreover, each
\gamma_{\lambda}
is constant, orbits

\mathbb{R}\cdot\bar{\alpha}_{\lambda}^{k}=S^{1}\cdot\bar{\alpha}_{\lambda}^{k}
are pairwise distinct, and
\bar{\alpha}_{\lambda}^{k}\notin\mathbb{R}\cdot\gamma_{\lambda}
as above.
Similarly,
\beta_{\lambda}^{1}
and
\beta_{\lambda}^{2}
are
\mathbb{R}
-distinct, and

\beta_{\lambda}^{1}\notin\mathbb{R}\cdot\gamma_{\lambda}
and
\beta_{\lambda}^{2}\notin\mathbb{R}\cdot\gamma_{\lambda}
.
Finally,
\mathfrak{E}_{\lambda}(\beta_{\lambda}^{1})\neq\mathfrak{E}_{\lambda}(\beta_{\lambda}^{2})
because
\check{\mathscr{L}}_{\lambda}(\hat{w}^{1}_{\lambda})\neq\check{\mathscr{L}}_{\lambda}(\hat{w}^{2}_{\lambda})
.
The desired assertions are proved.
\Box

3
Proofs of Theorems
1.6
,
1.7
,
1.8
,

1.9

3.1
Reduction of the problem (
1.4
) to one on open subsets of
\mathbb{R}^{n}

Since
\mathbb{I}_{g}(\bar{\gamma}(t))=\bar{\gamma}(t+\tau)\;\forall t\in\mathbb{R}
and

\mathbb{I}_{g}
is an isometry, the closure
Cl({\bar{\gamma}}(\mathbb{R}))
of
\bar{\gamma}(\mathbb{R})
is compact.
As in Section 3.1.1 in
[
27
]
or
[
28
]

we may choose a number
\iota>0
such that the following holds:

(
\clubsuit 6
)

the closure
\bar{\bf U}_{3\iota}(Cl({\bar{\gamma}}(\mathbb{R})))
of

{\bf U}_{3\iota}(Cl({\bar{\gamma}}(\mathbb{R}))):=\{p\in M\,|\,d_{g}(p,Cl({\bar{\gamma}}(\mathbb{R})))<3\iota\}
is
a compact neighborhood of
\gamma_{\mu}([0,\tau])
in
M
, and

\bar{\bf U}_{3\iota}(Cl({\bar{\gamma}}(\mathbb{R})))\times\bar{\bf U}_{3\iota}(Cl({\bar{\gamma}}(\mathbb{R})))

is contained in the image of
\mathbb{F}|_{\mathcal{W}(0_{TM})}
, where
\mathbb{F}
is given by the following (
3.1
).

(
\spadesuit 6
)

\{(q,v)\in TM\,|\,q\in\bar{\bf U}_{3\iota}(Cl({\bar{\gamma}}(\mathbb{R}))),\;|v|_{q}\leq 3\iota\}\subset\mathcal{W}(0_{TM})
.

Then
3\iota
is less than the injectivity
radius of
g
at each point on
\bar{\bf U}_{3\iota}(Cl({\bar{\gamma}}(\mathbb{R})))
.

Let us choose the
C^{6}
Riemannian metric
g
on
M
so that

S_{0}
(resp.
S_{1}
) is totally geodesic near
\gamma_{\mu}(0)
(resp.
\gamma_{\mu}(\tau)
).
There exists a fibrewise convex open neighborhood
\mathcal{U}(0_{TM})
of the zero section of
TM

such that the exponential map of
g
gives rise to
C^{5}
immersion

\mathbb{F}:\mathcal{U}(0_{TM})\to M\times M,\;(q,v)\mapsto(q,\exp_{q}(v)),

(3.1)

(cf. Appendix A in
[
27
]
or
[
28
]
).
By (A.2) in
[
27
]
or
[
28
]
,

d\mathbb{F}(q,0_{q}):T_{(q,0_{q})}\mathcal{U}(0_{TM})\to T_{(q,q)}(M\times M)=T_{q}M\times T_{q}M
is an isomorphism for each
q\in M
.
Since
\mathbb{F}
is injective on the closed subset
0_{TM}\subset TM
, it follows from Exercise 7 in

[
10
, page 41]
that
\mathbb{F}|_{\mathcal{W}(0_{TM})}
is a
C^{5}
embedding for some smaller open neighborhood

\mathcal{W}(0_{TM})\subset\mathcal{U}(0_{TM})
of
0_{TM}
.
Note that
\mathbb{F}(0_{TM})
is equal to the diagonal
\Delta_{M}
in

M\times M
, and that
\gamma_{\mu}([0,\tau])
is compact.

Since
\bar{\gamma}
is
C^{6}
, as in
[
20
, §3]
, starting with a unit orthogonal
frame at
T_{{\bar{\gamma}}(0)}M
and using the parallel
transport along
{\bar{\gamma}}
with respect to the Levi-Civita
connection of the Riemannian metric
g
we get a unit orthogonal
parallel
C^{5}
frame field
\mathbb{R}\to{\bar{\gamma}}^{\ast}TM,\;t\mapsto(e_{1}(t),\cdots,e_{n}(t))
,
such that

(e_{1}(t+\tau),\cdots,e_{n}(t+\tau))=(\mathbb{I}_{g\ast}(e_{1}(t)),\cdots,\mathbb{I}_{g\ast}(e_{n}(t)))E_{{\bar{\gamma}}}\quad\forall t\in\mathbb{R},

where
E_{{\bar{\gamma}}}
is an orthogonal matrix of order
n
.

By
[
11
, Corollary 2.5.11]

there exists an orthogonal matrix
\Xi
such that

\Xi^{-1}E_{\bar{\gamma}}\Xi={\rm diag}(S_{1},\cdots,S_{\sigma})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times n},

(3.2)

where each
S_{j}
is either
1
, or
-1
, or

{\scriptscriptstyle\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\cos\theta_{j}&\sin\theta_{j}\\
-\sin\theta_{j}&\cos\theta_{j}\end{array}\right)}
,
0<\theta_{j}<\pi
,
and their orders satisfy:
{\rm ord}(S_{1})\geq\cdots\geq{\rm ord}(S_{\sigma})
.
Replacing
(e_{1},\cdots,e_{n})
by
(e_{1},\cdots,e_{n})\Xi
we may assume

E_{\bar{\gamma}}={\rm diag}(S_{1},\cdots,S_{\sigma})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times n}.

(3.3)

Let
B^{n}_{r}(0):=\{x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\,|\,|x|<r\}
and
\bar{B}^{n}_{r}(0):=\{x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\,|\,|x|\leq r\}
for
r>0
.
Then

\displaystyle\phi_{{\bar{\gamma}}}:\mathbb{R}\times B^{n}_{3\iota}(0)\to M,\;(t,x)\mapsto\exp_{{\bar{\gamma}}(t)}\left(\sum^{n}_{i=1}x_{i}e_{i}(t)\right)

(3.4)

is a
C^{5}
map and satisfies

\displaystyle\phi_{{{\bar{\gamma}}}}(t+\tau,x)=\mathbb{I}_{g}\left(\phi_{{\bar{\gamma}}}(t,E_{{\bar{\gamma}}}x)\right)\quad\hbox{and}

\displaystyle d\phi_{{\bar{\gamma}}}(t+\tau,x)[(1,v)]=d\mathbb{I}_{g}\left(\phi_{{\bar{\gamma}}}(t,E_{{\bar{\gamma}}}x)\right)\circ d\phi_{{\bar{\gamma}}}(t,E_{{\bar{\gamma}}}x)[(1,E_{{\bar{\gamma}}}v)]

for any
(t,x,v)\in\mathbb{R}\times B^{n}_{3\iota}(0)\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
.
By
[
36
, Theorem 4.3]
, from
\phi_{{\bar{\gamma}}}
we get a
C^{2}
coordinate chart around
{\bar{\gamma}}
on the
C^{4}
Banach manifold

\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(M,\mathbb{I}_{g})

modeled on the Banach space

\mathcal{X}^{1}_{\tau}(\mathbb{R}^{n},{E}_{{\bar{\gamma}}})=\{\xi\in C^{1}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{R}^{n})\,|\,{E}^{T}_{{\bar{\gamma}}}\xi(t)=\xi(t+\tau)\;\forall t\in\mathbb{R}\}

with the induced norm
\|\xi\|_{C^{1}}
from
C^{1}([0,\tau],\mathbb{R}^{n})
,

\displaystyle\Phi_{{\bar{\gamma}}}:\mathcal{X}^{1}_{\tau}(B^{n}_{2\iota}(0),{E}_{{\bar{\gamma}}})=\{\xi\in\mathcal{X}^{1}_{\tau}(\mathbb{R}^{n},{E}_{{\bar{\gamma}}})\,|\,\|\xi\|_{C^{0}}<2\iota\}\to\mathcal{X}^{1}_{\tau}(M,\mathbb{I}_{g})

(3.5)

given by
\Phi_{{\bar{\gamma}}}(\xi)(t)=\phi_{{\bar{\gamma}}}(t,\xi(t))
.
Moreover

d\Phi_{{\bar{\gamma}}}(0):\mathcal{X}^{1}_{\tau}(\mathbb{R}^{n},{E}_{{\bar{\gamma}}})\to T_{\bar{\gamma}}\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(M,\mathbb{I}_{g}),\;\xi\mapsto\sum^{n}_{j=1}\xi_{j}e_{j}

is a Banach space isomorphism. (Actually, we have

\displaystyle|\xi(t)|^{2}_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}=\sum^{n}_{j=1}(\xi_{j}(t))^{2}=g\left(\sum^{n}_{j=1}\xi_{j}(t)e_{j}(t),\sum^{n}_{j=1}\xi_{j}(t)e_{j}(t)\right)=|d\Phi_{{\bar{\gamma}}}(0)[\xi](t)|^{2}_{g}

(3.6)

for any
\xi:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}^{n}
and
t\in\mathbb{R}
.) Therefore there exists a
unique
\zeta_{0}\in\mathcal{X}^{1}_{\tau}(\mathbb{R}^{n},{E}_{{\bar{\gamma}}})
satisfying

d\Phi_{{\bar{\gamma}}}(0)[\zeta_{0}]=\dot{\bar{\gamma}}
,
that is,
\dot{\bar{\gamma}}(t)=\sum^{n}_{j=1}\zeta_{0j}(t)e_{j}(t),\;\forall t\in{\mathbb{R}}
,
where

\zeta_{0j}(t)=g(\dot{\bar{\gamma}}(t),e_{j}(t))\;\forall t\in{\mathbb{R}},\quad j=1,\cdots,n.

Clearly,
\zeta_{0}\in\mathcal{X}^{5}_{\tau}(\mathbb{R}^{n},{E}_{{\bar{\gamma}}})
and
\zeta_{0}\neq 0
because
\bar{\gamma}
is nonconstant.
If
\gamma\in\mathcal{X}^{1}_{\tau}(M,\mathbb{I}_{g})
is nonconstant and
C^{l}
(
2\leq l\leq 5
),
by the arguments above
[
25
, Proposition 4.1]
the orbit
\mathcal{O}:=\mathbb{R}\cdot\gamma

is either a one-to-one
C^{l-1}
immersion submanifold of dimension one or a
C^{l-1}
-embedded circle; moreover

T_{\gamma}{\cal O}=\dot{\gamma}{\mathbb{R}}\subset T_{\gamma}\mathcal{X}^{1}_{\tau}(M,\mathbb{I}_{g})
.
Then for any reals
a<b
,
[a,b]\cdot\bar{\gamma}
is a
C^{4}
embedded submanifold of dimension one.
Take
a>0
such that
[-a,a]\cdot\bar{\gamma}\subset{\rm Im}(\Phi_{\bar{\gamma}})
. Then

S_{0}:=\Phi^{-1}_{{\bar{\gamma}}}\bigl([-a,a]\cdot\bar{\gamma}\cap{\rm Im}(\Phi_{\bar{\gamma}})\bigr)

(3.7)

is a one-dimensional compact
C^{2}
submanifold of
\mathcal{X}^{1}_{\tau}(B^{n}_{2\iota}(0),{E}_{{\bar{\gamma}}})

containing
0
as an interior point, and

T_{0}S_{0}=(d\Phi_{\bar{\gamma}}(0))^{-1}(T_{\bar{\gamma}}S_{0})=(d\Phi_{\bar{\gamma}}(0))^{-1}(\mathbb{R}\dot{\bar{\gamma}})={\mathbb{R}}\zeta_{0}.

Define the function
L^{\star}:\Lambda\times\mathbb{R}\times B^{n}_{3\iota}(0)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\to{\mathbb{R}}
by

\displaystyle L^{\star}(\lambda,t,x,v)=L\bigr(\lambda,\phi_{{\bar{\gamma}}}(t,x),d\phi_{{\bar{\gamma}}}(t,x)[(1,v)]\bigl).

It is continuous and satisfies

\displaystyle L^{\star}(\lambda,t+\tau,x,v)=L\bigr(\lambda,\phi_{{\bar{\gamma}}}(t+\tau,x),d\phi_{{\bar{\gamma}}}(t+\tau,x)[(1,v)]\bigl)

(3.8)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle L\left(\lambda,\mathbb{I}_{g}\left(\phi_{{\bar{\gamma}}}(t,E_{{\bar{\gamma}}}x)\right),d\mathbb{I}_{g}\left(\phi_{{\bar{\gamma}}}(t,E_{{\bar{\gamma}}}x)\right)\circ d\phi_{{\bar{\gamma}}}(t,E_{{\bar{\gamma}}}x)[(1,E_{{\bar{\gamma}}}v)]\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle L\left(\lambda,\phi_{{\bar{\gamma}}}(t,E_{{\bar{\gamma}}}x),d\phi_{{\bar{\gamma}}}(t,E_{{\bar{\gamma}}}x)[(1,E_{{\bar{\gamma}}}v)]\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle L^{\star}(\lambda,t,E_{{\bar{\gamma}}}x,E_{{\bar{\gamma}}}v)

for all
(\lambda,t,x,v)\in\Lambda\times\mathbb{R}\times B^{n}_{3\iota}(0)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
, and thus

\displaystyle\partial_{x}L^{\star}(\lambda,t+\tau,x,v)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E_{{\bar{\gamma}}}\partial_{x}L^{\star}\bigl(\lambda,t,E_{{\bar{\gamma}}}x,E_{{\bar{\gamma}}}v),

\displaystyle\partial_{v}L^{\star}(\lambda,t+\tau,x,v)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E_{{\bar{\gamma}}}\partial_{v}L^{\star}\bigl(\lambda,t,E_{{\bar{\gamma}}}x,E_{{\bar{\gamma}}}v)

for all
(\lambda,t,x,v)\in\Lambda\times\mathbb{R}\times B^{n}_{3\iota}(0)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
.
(Here
\partial_{x}L^{\star}
and
\partial_{v}L^{\star}
denote the gradients of
L^{\star}
with respect to
x
and
v
,
respectively. Recall that all vectors in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
in this paper are understood as column vectors.)
Each
L^{\star}(\lambda,\cdot)
is
C^{4}
and all its partial derivatives of order no more than two depend continuously on

(\lambda,t,x,v)\in\Lambda\times\mathbb{R}\times B^{n}_{3\iota}(0)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
.
Moreover,
d\phi_{{\bar{\gamma}}}(t,x)[(1,v)]=\partial_{x}\phi_{{\bar{\gamma}}}(t,x)[v]+\partial_{t}\phi_{{\bar{\gamma}}}(t,x)
implies that

\mathbb{R}^{n}\ni v\mapsto L^{\star}(\lambda,t,x,v)=L\bigr(\lambda,\phi_{{\bar{\gamma}}}(t,x),d\phi_{{\bar{\gamma}}}(t,x)[(1,v)]\bigl)\in\mathbb{R}

is strictly convex.

The
C^{2}
functional
\mathfrak{E}_{\lambda}
in (
1.5
) gives rise to a
C^{2}

functional
\mathfrak{E}^{\star}_{\lambda}:\mathcal{X}^{1}_{\tau}(B^{n}_{2\iota}(0),{E}_{\bar{\gamma}})\to\mathbb{R}
,

\mathfrak{E}^{\star}_{\lambda}(\xi)=\int^{\tau}_{0}L^{\star}(\lambda,t,\xi(t),\dot{\xi}(t))dt=\mathfrak{E}_{\lambda}\circ\Phi_{{\bar{\gamma}}}(\xi).

(3.9)

Since
\mathfrak{E}_{\lambda}
is invariant under the continuous
\mathbb{R}
-action on

\mathcal{X}^{1}_{\tau}(M,\mathbb{I}_{g})
given by

\chi:\mathcal{X}^{1}_{\tau}(M,\mathbb{I}_{g})\times\mathbb{R}\to\mathcal{X}^{1}_{\tau}(M,\mathbb{I}_{g}),\;(s,\gamma)\mapsto s\cdot\gamma,

(3.10)

where
(s\cdot\gamma)(t)=\gamma(s+t)\;\forall s,t\in\mathbb{R}
,

S_{0}
is a critical submanifold of each
\mathfrak{E}^{\star}_{\lambda}
,
and there holds

D^{2}\mathfrak{E}^{\star}_{\lambda}(0)[\xi,\eta]=D^{2}\mathfrak{E}_{\lambda}(\bar{\gamma})\big[d\Phi_{{\bar{\gamma}}}(0)[\xi],d\Phi_{{\bar{\gamma}}}(0)[\eta]\big]\quad\forall\xi,\eta\in\mathcal{X}^{1}_{\tau}(B^{n}_{2\iota}(0),{E}_{\bar{\gamma}}),

which implies

m^{-}_{\tau}(\mathfrak{E}^{\star}_{\lambda},0)=m^{-}_{\tau}(\mathfrak{E}_{\lambda},\bar{\gamma})\quad\hbox{and}\quad m^{0}_{\tau}(\mathfrak{L}^{\star}_{\lambda},0)=m^{0}_{\tau}(\mathfrak{E}_{\lambda},\bar{\gamma}).

(3.11)

Since
S_{0}
is compact, there exists
\rho_{0}>3\iota
such that

\sup_{t}|\dot{x}(t)|<\rho_{0}
for all
x\in S_{0}
.
For the Lagrangian
L^{\star}
, as in Lemma 3.8 in
[
27
]
or
[
28
]

we can modify it to obtain:

Lemma 3.1
.

For a given subset
\hat{\Lambda}\subset\Lambda
which is either compact or sequential compact,
there exists a continuous function
\check{L}:\hat{\Lambda}\times\mathbb{R}\times\bar{B}^{n}_{2\iota}(0)\times\mathbb{R}^{n}\to{\mathbb{R}}

satisfying the following conditions
for some constants
\kappa>0
and
0<c<C
:

(L0)

\check{L}(\lambda,t+\tau,x,v)=\check{L}(\lambda,t,E_{{\bar{\gamma}}}x,E_{{\bar{\gamma}}}v)

for all
(\lambda,t,x,v)
, each function

\check{L}_{\lambda}(\cdot)=\check{L}(\lambda,\cdot)
(
\lambda\in\hat{\Lambda}
) is
C^{4}
and partial derivatives

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\check{L}_{\lambda}(\cdot),\quad\partial_{q}\check{L}_{\lambda}(\cdot),\quad\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda}(\cdot),\quad\partial_{qv}\check{L}_{\lambda}(\cdot),\quad\partial_{qq}\check{L}_{\lambda}(\cdot),\quad\partial_{vv}\check{L}_{\lambda}(\cdot)

\displaystyle\hbox{and}\quad\partial_{tt}\check{L}_{\lambda}(\cdot),\quad\partial_{tq}\check{L}_{\lambda}(\cdot),\quad\partial_{tv}\check{L}_{\lambda}(\cdot)

depend continuously on
(\lambda,t,q,v)\in\hat{\Lambda}\times\mathbb{R}\times\bar{B}^{n}_{2\iota}(0)\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
.

(L1)

\check{L}
and
{L}^{\star}
are same on

\hat{\Lambda}\times\mathbb{R}\times B^{n}_{3\iota/2}(0)\times B^{n}_{\rho_{0}}(0)
;

(L2)

\partial_{vv}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,q,v)\geq cI_{n},\quad\forall(\lambda,t,q,v)\in\hat{\Lambda}\times[0,\tau]\times B^{n}_{3\iota/2}(0)\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
.

(L3)

\Bigl|\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial q_{i}\partial q_{j}}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,q,v)\Bigr|\leq C(1+|v|^{2}),\quad\Bigl|\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial q_{i}\partial v_{j}}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,q,v)\Bigr|\leq C(1+|v|),\quad\hbox{and}\\
\Bigl|\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial v_{i}\partial v_{j}}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,q,v)\Bigr|\leq C,\quad\forall(\lambda,t,q,v)\in\hat{\Lambda}\times[0,\tau]\times B^{n}_{3\iota/2}(0)\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
.

(L4)

\check{L}(\lambda,t,q,v)\geq\kappa|v|^{2}-C,\quad\forall(\lambda,t,q,v)\in\hat{\Lambda}\times\mathbb{R}\times B^{n}_{3\iota/2}(0)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
.

(L5)

|\partial_{q}\check{L}(\lambda,t,q,v)|\leq C(1+|v|^{2})
and
|\partial_{q}\check{L}(\lambda,t,q,v)|\leq C(1+|v|)

for all
(\lambda,t,q,v)\in\hat{\Lambda}\times\mathbb{R}\times B^{n}_{3\iota/2}(0)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
.

(L6)

|\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,q,v)|\leq C(1+|v|^{2}),\quad\forall(\lambda,t,q,v)\in\hat{\Lambda}\times\mathbb{R}\times\bar{B}^{n}_{3\iota/2}(0)\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
.

Consider the Banach space

{\bf X}:=\mathcal{X}^{1}_{\tau}(\mathbb{R}^{n},{E}_{{\bar{\gamma}}})

with the induced norm
\|\cdot\|_{C^{1}}
from
C^{1}([0,\tau],\mathbb{R}^{n})
, and
the Hilbert space

{\bf H}:=\{\xi\in W^{1,2}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{R}^{n})\,|\,{E}^{T}_{\bar{\gamma}}(\xi(t))=\xi(t+\tau)\;\forall t\in\mathbb{R}\}

equipped with
W^{1,2}
-inner product as in (
2.5
).
Both carry a natural
\mathbb{R}
-action given by
(\theta\cdot x)(t)=x(t+\theta)

for
\theta\in\mathbb{R}
. The spaces
{\bf H}
and
{\bf X}

have the following
\mathbb{R}
-invariant open subsets

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}:

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{\xi\in W^{1,2}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R};B^{n}_{3\iota/2}(0))\,|\,{E}^{T}_{\bar{\gamma}}(\xi(t))=\xi(t+\tau)\;\forall t\in\mathbb{R}\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}^{X}:

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}\cap{\bf X}=\mathcal{X}^{1}_{\tau}(B^{n}_{3\iota/2}(0),{E}_{{\bar{\gamma}}})

respectively. Define a family of functionals
\check{\mathscr{L}}_{\lambda}:\mathcal{U}\to{\mathbb{R}}
by

\check{\mathscr{L}}_{\lambda}(x)=\int^{\tau}_{0}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,x(t),\dot{x}(t))dt,\quad\lambda\in\hat{\Lambda},

(3.12)

and put
{\bf H}^{\bot}:=\{x\in{\bf H}\,|\,(\zeta_{0},x)_{1,2}=0\}
and

\displaystyle{\bf X}^{\bot}:=\{x\in{\bf X}\,|\,(\zeta_{0},x)_{1,2}=0\}={\bf X}\cap{\bf H}^{\bot},

(3.13)

\displaystyle{\check{\mathscr{L}}}_{\lambda}^{\bot}:\mathcal{U}\cap{\bf H}^{\bot}\to{\mathbb{R}},\;x\mapsto\check{\mathscr{L}}_{\lambda}(x).

(3.14)

Remark 3.2
.

Because of Lemma
3.1
, by
[
3
, Theorem 4.5]
we deduce that
every critical point of
\check{\mathscr{L}}_{\lambda}
is
C^{4}
.

3.2
Properties of functionals
\check{\mathscr{L}}
and
\check{\mathscr{L}}^{\bot}_{\lambda}

In order to use the abstract theorems developed in
[
22
,
24
,
25
]
we need to study some
properties of the functionals
\check{\mathscr{L}}
and
\check{\mathscr{L}}^{\bot}_{\lambda}
near
0
.
By
[
20
, §3]
and
[
18
]
, we have (i)-(iii) of the following corresponding result of
Proposition 3.9 in
[
27
]
or
[
28
]
.

Proposition 3.3
.

(i)

\check{\mathscr{L}}_{\lambda}
is
C^{2-0}
and the gradient map
\nabla\check{\mathscr{L}}_{\lambda}:\mathcal{U}\to{\bf H}

has the Gâteaux derivative
{B}_{\lambda}(x)\in{\mathscr{L}}_{s}({\bf H})
at
x\in\mathcal{U}
.

(ii)

\nabla\check{\mathscr{L}}_{\lambda}
restricts to a
C^{1}
map
A_{\lambda}:\mathcal{U}^{X}\to{\bf X}
.

(iii)

(D1) of
[
22
, Hypothesis 1.1]
and (C) of
[
22
, Hypothesis 1.3]
hold near the origin
0\in{\bf H}
, i.e.,

\{u\in{\bf H}\,|\,{B}_{\lambda}(0)u=su,\,s\leq 0\}\subset{\bf X}\quad\hbox{and}\quad\{u\in{\bf H}\,|\,{B}_{\lambda}(0)u\in X\}\subset{\bf X}.

(3.15)

(iv)

Since
\check{L}=\tilde{L}=L^{\star}
on
\Lambda\times\mathbb{R}\times{B}^{n}_{3\iota/2}(0)\times B^{n}_{\rho_{0}}(0)
, it holds that

\check{\mathscr{L}}_{\lambda}(x)=\int^{\tau}_{0}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,x(t),\dot{x}(t))dt=\mathscr{L}^{\star}_{\lambda}(x)

(3.16)

for each
x
in an open subset
\{x\in\mathcal{X}^{1}_{\tau}(B^{n}_{3\iota/2}(0),{E}_{{\bar{\gamma}}})\,|\,\sup_{t}|\dot{x}(t)|<\rho_{0}\}

of
\mathcal{U}^{X}
. Clearly,
0\in S_{0}
has an open neighborhood

S_{00}
in
S_{0}
contained in the open subset.
It follows that
d\check{L}_{\lambda}(x)=0\;\forall x\in S_{00}
and that

m^{-}_{\tau}(\check{\mathscr{L}}_{\lambda},0)=m^{-}_{\tau}(\mathfrak{E}^{\star}_{\lambda},0)\quad\hbox{and}\quad m^{0}_{\tau}(\check{\mathscr{L}}_{\lambda},0)=m^{0}_{\tau}(\mathfrak{E}^{\star}_{\lambda},0).

(3.17)

By (iv) and (
3.9
),
a point
x\in\mathcal{X}^{1}_{\tau}(B^{n}_{3\iota/2}(0),{E}_{{\bar{\gamma}}})
near
0

is a critical point of
\mathfrak{E}^{\star}_{\lambda}
if and only if

\gamma=\Phi_{{\bar{\gamma}}}(x)\in\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(M,\mathbb{I}_{g})

is a critical point of
\mathfrak{E}_{\lambda}
near
\bar{\gamma}
. However,
if
\gamma\in{\rm Im}(\Phi_{\bar{\gamma}})
is a critical point of
\mathfrak{E}_{\lambda}
,
so is each point in
\mathbb{R}\cdot\gamma
. Therefore for
|s|
small enough
(\Phi_{\bar{\gamma}})^{-1}(s\cdot\gamma)

is also a critical point of
\mathfrak{E}^{\star}_{\lambda}
.
Such a critical point is said to be the
\mathbb{R}
-same as
\gamma
.
We need to study behavior of
\mathbb{R}
-distinct critical
points of
\mathfrak{E}^{\star}_{\lambda}
near
0
.
Clearly,
d\check{\mathscr{L}}_{\lambda}(0)=0
and

d\check{\mathscr{L}}_{\lambda}^{\bot}(0)=0\;\forall\lambda
.

Let
\Pi:{\bf H}\to{\bf H}^{\bot}
be the orthogonal projection.
Then
\Pi(x)=x-\frac{(x,\zeta_{0})_{1,2}}{\|\zeta_{0}\|_{1,2}}\zeta_{0}
for
x\in{\bf H}
, and

\nabla{\check{\mathscr{L}}}_{\lambda}^{\bot}(x)=\nabla\check{\mathscr{L}}_{\lambda}(x)-\frac{(\nabla\check{\mathscr{L}}_{\lambda}(x),\zeta_{0})_{1,2}}{\|\zeta_{0}\|_{1,2}}\zeta_{0}\quad\forall x\in\mathcal{U}\cap{\bf H}^{\bot},

(3.18)

where by
[
20
, (3.10)-(3.11)]
(all vectors
in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
are understood as column vectors now)

\displaystyle\nabla\check{\mathscr{L}}_{\lambda}(\xi)(t)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int^{\infty}_{t}e^{t-s}\left[\partial_{q}\check{L}_{\lambda}\bigl(s,\xi(s),\dot{\xi}(s)\bigr)-\mathfrak{R}^{\xi}_{\lambda}(s)\right]\,ds

(3.19)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int_{-\infty}^{t}e^{s-t}\left[\partial_{q}\check{L}_{{\lambda}}\bigl(s,\xi(s),\dot{\xi}(s)\bigr)-\mathfrak{R}^{\xi}(s)\right]\,ds+\mathfrak{R}^{\xi}_{\lambda}(t)

where
\mathfrak{R}^{\xi}_{\lambda}
, (provided
2={\rm ord}(S_{p})>{\rm ord}(S_{p+1})
for some

p\in\{0,\cdots,\sigma\}
in (
3.3
)), is given by

\displaystyle\mathfrak{R}_{\lambda}^{\xi}(t)=\int^{t}_{0}\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda}\bigl(s,\xi(s),\dot{\xi}(s)\bigr)ds+

\displaystyle\left[\left(\oplus_{l\leq p}\frac{\sin\theta_{l}}{2-2\cos\theta_{l}}{\scriptscriptstyle\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&-1\\
1&0\end{array}\right)}-\frac{1}{2}I_{2p}\right)\oplus{\rm diag}(a_{p+1}(t),\cdots,a_{\sigma}(t))\right]\int^{\tau}_{0}\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda}\bigl(s,\xi(s),\dot{\xi}(s)\bigr)ds

with
a_{j}(t)=\frac{2t{S}_{j}+2t+1-{S}_{j}}{4}
,

j=p+1,\cdots,{\sigma}=n-p
. (As usual
p=0
(resp.
p={\sigma}
) means that

{\rm ord}(S_{1})=\cdots={\rm ord}(S_{\sigma})=1
(resp.
{\rm ord}(S_{1})=\cdots={\rm ord}(S_{\sigma})=2
)
and hence there is no the first (resp. second) term in the square brackets in (
3.2
).) Note that

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\mathfrak{R}_{\lambda}^{\xi}(t)=\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda}\bigl(t,\xi(t),\dot{\xi}(t)\bigr)+\mathfrak{M}\int^{\tau}_{0}\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda}\bigl(s,\xi(s),\dot{\xi}(s)\bigr)ds,

(3.24)

where
\mathfrak{M}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
is a matrix only depending on
E
.

Since
\zeta_{0}
is
C^{1}
, we derive from (
3.18
) that
\nabla{\check{\mathscr{L}}}_{\lambda}^{\bot}(x)\in{\bf X}^{\bot}

for any
x\in{\bf X}^{\bot}\cap\mathcal{U}
, and

\mathbb{A}_{\lambda}:\mathcal{U}^{X}\cap{\bf X}^{\bot}\to{\bf X}^{\bot},\;x\mapsto\nabla{\check{\mathscr{L}}}_{\lambda}^{\bot}(x)

(3.25)

is
C^{1}
. By
[
20
, §3]

\nabla{\check{\mathscr{L}}}_{\lambda}^{\bot}

has the Gâteaux derivative
\mathbb{B}_{\lambda}(x)\in{\mathscr{L}}_{s}({\bf H}^{\bot})
at
x\in\mathcal{U}\cap{\bf H}^{\bot}

given by

\mathbb{B}_{\lambda}(x)v=B_{\lambda}(x)v-\frac{(B_{\lambda}(x)v,\zeta_{0})_{2}}{\|\zeta_{0}\|_{2}}\zeta_{0}\quad\forall v\in{\bf H}^{\bot},

(3.26)

where
B_{\lambda}(\zeta)\in{\mathscr{L}}_{s}({\bf H})
for
\zeta\in\mathcal{U}
is a self-adjoint Fredholm
operator defined by (
2.13
).
Let
{B}_{\lambda}=\textsl{{P}}_{{\lambda}}+\textsl{{Q}}_{{\lambda}}

be the decomposition as in Proposition
2.1
(v).
Then

\mathbb{P}_{\lambda}(x):=\Pi\circ\textsl{P}_{\lambda}(x)|_{{\bf H}^{\bot}}\quad\hbox{and}\quad\mathbb{Q}_{\lambda}(x):=\Pi\circ\textsl{Q}_{\lambda}(x)|_{{\bf H}^{\bot}}

(3.27)

are positive definite and compact, respectively, and
\mathbb{B}_{\lambda}(x)=\mathbb{P}_{\lambda}(x)+\mathbb{Q}_{\lambda}(x)

by (
3.26
).
Since
{\mathbb{R}}\zeta_{0}\subset{\rm Ker}(B_{\lambda}(0))
we get

\displaystyle m^{-}_{\tau}({\check{\mathscr{L}}}_{\lambda}^{\bot},0)=m^{-}_{\tau}({\check{\mathscr{L}}}_{\lambda},0)\quad\hbox{and}\quad m^{0}_{\tau}({\check{\mathscr{L}}}_{\lambda}^{\bot},0)=m^{0}_{\tau}({\check{\mathscr{L}}}_{\lambda},0)-1.

(3.28)

Clearly, (L1) and (
2.14
) yield

\displaystyle(\textsl{P}_{{\lambda}}(\zeta)\xi,\xi)_{1,2}\geq\min\{c,1\}\|\xi\|_{1,2}^{2},\quad\forall x\in\mathcal{U},\;\forall\xi\in{\bf H},

(3.29)

and hence

\displaystyle(\mathbb{P}_{{\lambda}}(x)\xi,\xi)_{1,2}\geq\min\{c,1\}\|\xi\|_{1,2}^{2},\quad\forall\xi\in{\bf H}^{\bot},\quad\forall x\in\mathcal{U}\cap{\bf H}^{\bot}.

(3.30)

Proposition 3.4
.

Let
(\lambda_{k})\subset\Lambda
and
(\zeta_{k})\subset\mathcal{U}

converge to
\mu\in\Lambda
and
0\in\mathcal{U}
, respectively.
Then
\|\textsl{P}_{{\lambda_{k}}}(\zeta_{k})\xi-\textsl{P}_{\mu}(0)\xi\|_{1,2}\to 0
for each
\xi\in H
.
In particular, if
(\zeta_{k})\subset\mathcal{U}\cap{\bf H}^{\bot}
converges to zero, then

\|\mathbb{P}_{\lambda_{k}}(\zeta_{k})\xi-\mathbb{P}_{\mu}(0)\xi\|_{1,2}\to 0
for any
\xi\in{\bf H}^{\bot}
.

Proof.

By (
2.14
) we have

\displaystyle\|[\textsl{P}_{{\lambda_{k}}}(\zeta_{k})-\textsl{P}_{{\mu}}(0)]\xi\|^{2}_{1,2}\leq\int_{0}^{\tau}\big|[\partial_{vv}\check{L}_{{\lambda_{k}}}\bigl(t,\zeta_{k}(t),\dot{\zeta}_{k}(t)\bigr)-\partial_{vv}\check{L}_{\mu}\bigl(t,0,0\bigr)]\dot{\xi}(t)\big|^{2}_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\,dt.

Note that
\|\zeta_{k}\|_{1,2}\to 0
implies
\|\zeta_{k}\|_{C^{0}}\to 0
.
Since
(\lambda,t,x,v)\mapsto\partial_{vv}\check{L}_{{\lambda}}(t,x,v)
is continuous, by
the third inequality in (L2) in Lemma
3.1
we may apply
[
23
, Prop. C.1]
to

f(t,\eta;\lambda)=\partial_{vv}\check{L}(\lambda,t,\zeta_{k}(t),\dot{\zeta}_{k}(t))\eta

to get that

\int_{0}^{\tau}\big|[\partial_{vv}\check{L}_{{\lambda_{k}}}\bigl(t,\zeta_{k}(t),\dot{\zeta}_{k}(t)\bigr)-\partial_{vv}\check{L}_{{\mu}}\bigl(t,0,0\bigr)]\dot{\xi}(t)\big|^{2}_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\,dt\to 0.

Moreover, the Lebesgue dominated convergence theorem also leads to

\int_{0}^{\tau}\big|[\partial_{vv}\check{L}_{{\lambda_{k}}}\bigl(t,0,0\bigr)-\partial_{vv}\check{L}_{{\mu}}\bigl(t,0,0\bigr)]\dot{\xi}(t)\big|^{2}_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\,dt\to 0.

Hence
\|[\textsl{P}_{{\lambda_{k}}}(\zeta_{k})-\textsl{P}_{\mu}(0)]\xi\|_{1,2}\to 0
.
The final claim follows from this and (
3.27
).
∎

Proposition 3.5
.

\mathcal{U}\ni\zeta\mapsto\textsl{Q}_{{\lambda}}(\zeta)\in\mathscr{L}_{s}({\bf H})

is uniformly continuous at
0
with respect to
\lambda\in\Lambda
.
Moreover, if
(\lambda_{k})\subset\Lambda
converges to
0\in\Lambda
then

\|\textsl{Q}_{\lambda_{k}}(0)-\textsl{Q}_{\mu}(0)\|\to 0
.

Proof.

Write
\textsl{Q}_{\lambda}(\zeta):=\textsl{Q}_{\lambda,1}(\zeta)+\textsl{Q}_{\lambda,2}(\zeta)+\textsl{Q}_{\lambda,3}(\zeta)
, where

\displaystyle(\textsl{Q}_{\lambda,1}(\zeta)\xi,\eta)_{1,2}=\int_{0}^{\tau}\partial_{vq}\check{L}_{\lambda}\big(t,\zeta(t),\dot{\zeta}(t)\big)[\dot{\xi}(t),\eta(t)]dt,

\displaystyle(\textsl{Q}_{\lambda,2}(\zeta)\xi,\eta)_{1,2}=\int_{0}^{\tau}\partial_{qv}\check{L}_{\lambda}\big(t,\zeta(t),\dot{\zeta}(t)\big)[\xi(t),\dot{\eta}(t)]dt,

\displaystyle(\textsl{Q}_{\lambda,3}(\zeta)\xi,\eta)_{1,2}=\int_{0}^{\tau}\bigl(\partial_{qq}\check{L}_{\lambda}\big(t,\zeta(t),\dot{\zeta}(t)\big)[\xi(t),\eta(t)]-\bigl(\xi(t),\eta(t)\bigr)_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\bigr)\,dt.

As above the first claim follows from (L2) in Lemma
3.1
and
[
23
, Prop. C.1]
directly.

In order to prove the second claim, as in the proof of
[
18
, page 571]
we have

\displaystyle\|\textsl{Q}_{\lambda_{k},1}(0)-\textsl{Q}_{\mu,1}(0)\|_{\mathscr{L}({\bf H})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2(e^{\tau}+1)\left(\int^{\tau}_{0}\big|\partial_{vq}\check{L}_{\lambda_{k}}(s,0,0)-\partial_{vq}\check{L}_{\mu}(s,0,0)\big|^{2}ds\right)^{1/2}.

Because of the second inequality in (
L2
), it follows from the Lebesgue dominated convergence theorem
that
\|\textsl{Q}_{\lambda_{k},1}(0)-\textsl{Q}_{\mu,1}(0)\|_{\mathscr{L}({\bf H})}\to 0
.
Observe that

(\textsl{Q}_{\lambda,2}(\zeta)\xi,\eta)_{1,2}=\bigr(\xi,(\textsl{Q}_{\lambda,1}(\zeta))^{\ast}\eta\bigl)_{1,2}
.
Hence
\|\textsl{Q}_{\lambda_{k},2}(0)-\textsl{Q}_{\mu,2}(0)\|_{\mathscr{L}({\bf H})}\to 0
.
Finally, it is easy to deduce that

\displaystyle\|\textsl{Q}_{\lambda_{k},3}(0)-\textsl{Q}_{\mu,3}(0)\|^{2}_{\mathscr{L}({\bf H})}\leq\int_{0}^{\tau}\bigl|\partial_{qq}\check{L}_{\lambda_{k}}\big(t,0,0\big)-\partial_{qq}\check{L}_{\mu}\big(t,0,0\big)\bigr|^{2}\,dt.

By the Lebesgue dominated convergence theorem the right side converges to zero.
Then
\|\textsl{Q}_{\lambda_{k},3}(0)-\textsl{Q}_{\mu,3}(0)\|_{\mathscr{L}({\bf H})}\to 0
and therefore

\|\textsl{Q}_{\lambda_{k}}(0)-\textsl{Q}_{\mu}(0)\|\to 0
.
∎

By (
3.27
) and Proposition
3.5
, the map

\mathcal{U}\cap{\bf H}^{\bot}\ni\zeta\mapsto\mathbb{Q}_{{\lambda}}(\zeta)\in\mathscr{L}_{s}({\bf H}^{\bot})

is uniformly continuous at
0
with respect to
\lambda\in\Lambda
.
Moreover, if
(\lambda_{k})\subset\Lambda
converges to
0\in\Lambda
then

\|\mathbb{Q}_{\lambda_{k}}(0)-\mathbb{Q}_{\mu}(0)\|\to 0
.

Proposition 3.6
.

({\bf H}^{\bot},{\bf X}^{\bot},{\check{\mathscr{L}}}_{\lambda}^{\bot},\mathbb{A}_{\lambda}=\nabla{\check{\mathscr{L}}}_{\lambda}^{\bot},\mathbb{B}_{\lambda})

satisfies (C) of
[
25
, Hypothesis C.3]
(
[
22
, Hypothesis 1.3]
) and (D1) of
[
25
, Hypothesis C.3]
(
[
22
, Hypothesis 1.1]
)
at the origin
0\in{\bf H}^{\bot}
, namely

(C)

\{u\in{\bf H}^{\bot}\,|\,\mathbb{B}_{\lambda}(0)u\in{\bf X}^{\bot}\}\subset{\bf X}^{\bot}
,

(D1)

\{u\in{\bf H}^{\bot}\,|\,\mathbb{B}_{\lambda}(0)u=su,\;s\leq 0\}\subset{\bf X}^{\bot}
.

Proof.

In order to prove (C) let
u\in{\bf H}^{\bot}
be such that
v:=\mathbb{B}_{\lambda}(0)u\in{\bf X}^{\bot}
. By (
3.26
)

B_{\lambda}(0)u-\frac{(B_{\lambda}(0)u,\zeta_{0})_{2}}{\|\zeta_{0}\|_{2}}\zeta_{0}=\mathbb{B}_{\lambda}(0)u=v\in{\bf X}^{\bot}.

Since
\zeta_{0}\in{\bf X}
, it follows from this and (
3.15
)
that
u\in{\bf X}
and hence
u\in{\bf X}^{\bot}
.

Next, let
u\in{\bf H}^{\bot}
satisfy
\mathbb{B}_{\lambda}(0)u=su
for some
s\leq 0
. Then (
3.26
) leads to

B_{\lambda}(0)u=su+\frac{(B_{\lambda}(0)u,\zeta_{0})_{2}}{\|\zeta_{0}\|_{2}}\zeta_{0}.

Since
T_{0}S_{0}=\mathbb{R}\zeta_{0}
and
\nabla{\check{\mathscr{L}}}_{\lambda}(x)=0\,\forall x\in S_{0}
,
we deduce
B_{\lambda}(0)\zeta_{0}=0
and therefore

B_{\lambda}(0)u=su
. By (
3.15
) this implies
u\in{\bf X}
and so
u\in{\bf X}^{\bot}
.
∎

We also need the following two corresponding results of Propositions 3.10, 3.11 in
[
27
]

or
[
28
]
.

Proposition 3.7
.

Both
\hat{\Lambda}\times\mathcal{U}\ni(\lambda,x)\mapsto\check{\mathscr{L}}_{\lambda}(x)\in{\mathbb{R}}
and

\hat{\Lambda}\times\mathcal{U}^{X}\ni(\lambda,x)\mapsto A_{\lambda}(x)\in{\bf X}
are continuous.

Proofs of this proposition and the following key result are similar to
those of Propositions 3.10, 3.11 in
[
27
]
or
[
28
]
,
respectively. For completeness their proof are put off until Section
3.4

because they are rather long.

Proposition 3.8
.

Let
\bar{\varepsilon}>0
be such that
B_{{\bf H}}(0,\bar{\varepsilon})\subset\mathcal{U}
.
For any given
\epsilon>0
there exists
0<\varepsilon\leq\bar{\varepsilon}
such that
if
x\in B_{{\bf H}^{\bot}}(0,\varepsilon):=\{x\,|\,x\in{\bf H}^{\bot},\;\|x\|_{1,2}<\varepsilon\}
is a critical point of

\check{\mathscr{L}}^{\bot}_{\lambda}
with some
\lambda\in\hat{\Lambda}

then
x
is a critical point of
\check{\mathscr{L}}_{\lambda}
, belongs to
C^{4}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{R}^{n})

and also satisfies
\|x\|_{C^{2}}<\epsilon
. In particular,
for
x\in B_{{\bf H}^{\bot}}(0,\varepsilon)
,

d{\check{\mathscr{L}}}_{\lambda}^{\bot}(x)=0
if and only if
d{\check{\mathscr{L}}}_{\lambda}(x)=0
.

3.3
Completing the proofs of Theorems
1.6
,
1.7
,

1.8
,
1.9

The ideas are the same as the proofs of
[
25
, Theorems 1.18, 1.19, 1.21]
.
But the corresponding checks and computations are much more complex and difficult.

Proposition 3.9

(
[
2
, Proposition 3.5]
)
.

Let
P
be a finite-dimensional manifold,
N
a (possibly infinite dimensional)
Banach manifold,
Q\subset N
a Banach submanifold, and
A
a topological
space. Assume that
\chi:A\times P\to N
is a continuous function such that there exist

a_{0}\in A
and
m_{0}\in P
with:

(a)

\chi(a_{0},m_{0})\in Q
;

(b)

\chi(a_{0},\cdot):P\to N
is of class
C^{1}
;

(c)

\partial_{2}\chi(a_{0},m_{0})(T_{m_{0}}P)+T_{\chi(a_{0},m_{0})}Q=T_{\chi(a_{0},m_{0})}N
.

Then, for
a\in A
near
a_{0}
,
\chi(a,P)\cap Q\neq\emptyset
.

For
0<\delta\leq 3\iota/2
put

B_{{\bf X}^{\bot}}(0,\delta):=\{\xi\in C^{1}_{{E}_{{\bar{\gamma}}},\tau}(\mathbb{R},B^{n}_{3\iota/2}(0))\,|\,\|\xi\|_{C^{1}}<\delta\}\quad\hbox{and}\quad\Omega_{\delta}:=\Phi_{\bar{\gamma}}\left(B_{{\bf X}^{\bot}}(0,\delta)\right).

Clearly,
\Omega_{\delta}

is a
C^{2}
Banach submanifold of
\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(M,\mathbb{I}_{g})
.
For the action
\chi
in (
3.10
),
since
\bar{\gamma}
is nonconstant and
C^{6}
,

\chi(\bar{\gamma},\cdot):\mathbb{R}\to\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(M,\mathbb{I}_{g}),\;s\mapsto\chi(\bar{\gamma},s)

is a
C^{4}
one-to-one immersion, and

\partial_{2}\chi(\bar{\gamma},0)(T_{0}\mathbb{R})=\mathbb{R}\dot{\bar{\gamma}}=d\Phi_{\bar{\gamma}}(0)(\mathbb{R}\zeta_{0})\quad\hbox{and}\quad T_{\chi(\bar{\gamma},0)}\Omega_{\delta}=T_{\bar{\gamma}}\Omega_{\delta}=d\Phi_{\bar{\gamma}}(0)({\bf X}^{\bot}),

we have
\partial_{2}\chi(\bar{\gamma},0)(T_{0}\mathbb{R})+T_{\chi(\bar{\gamma},0)}\Omega_{\delta}=T_{\chi(\bar{\gamma},)}\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(M,\mathbb{I}_{g})
.
Applying Proposition
3.9
to
A=N=\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(M,\mathbb{I}_{g})
,
P={\mathbb{R}}
,
Q=\Omega_{\delta}
,

a_{0}=\bar{\gamma}
,
m_{0}=0
we get:

Proposition 3.10
.

For any given
0<\delta\leq 2\iota
, if
\gamma\in\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(M,\mathbb{I}_{g})

is close to
\bar{\gamma}
, then

({\mathbb{R}}\cdot\gamma)\cap\Omega_{\delta}\neq\emptyset
, that is,

{\mathbb{R}}\cdot\Omega_{\delta}
is a neighborhood of the orbit
\mathcal{O}={\mathbb{R}}\cdot\bar{\gamma}
in
\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(M,\mathbb{I}_{g})
.

Proof of Theorem
1.6
.

By the assumptions there exists a sequence
(\lambda_{k})\subset\Lambda

converging to
\mu\in\Lambda
such that the problem (
1.4
)
with
\lambda=\lambda_{k}
has solutions
\gamma_{k}
,
k=1,2,\cdots
,
which are
{\mathbb{R}}
-distinct each other and satisfy

\gamma_{k}|_{[0,\tau]}\to\bar{\gamma}|_{[0,\tau]}
in
C^{1}([0,\tau];M)
.
Then
\hat{\Lambda}=\{\mu,\lambda_{k}\,|\,k\in\mathbb{N}\}
is compact and sequential compact.
Take a decreasing sequence of positive numbers
\delta_{m}\leq 2\iota
such that
\delta_{m}\to 0
.
For each
\delta_{m}
, by Proposition
3.10
we have

\gamma_{k_{m}}\in{\mathbb{R}}\cdot\Omega_{\delta_{m}}
and thus

\beta_{m}:=s_{m}\cdot\gamma_{k_{m}}\in\Omega_{\delta_{m}}
for some
s_{m}\in{\mathbb{R}}
.
Note that each
\beta_{m}
is a critical point of
{{\mathfrak{E}}}_{\lambda_{k_{m}}}
on

\Phi_{\bar{\gamma}}(\mathcal{U}^{X})
. Since any two of
(\gamma_{k})
are
{\mathbb{R}}
-distinct,
so are any two of
(\beta_{m})
.

Note that
\Omega_{\delta}\subset\Phi_{\bar{\gamma}}\left(\mathcal{U}^{X}\right)\subset\Phi_{\bar{\gamma}}\big(C^{1}_{{E}_{{\bar{\gamma}}},\tau}(\mathbb{R},B^{n}_{3\iota/2}(0))\big)
,
and by (
3.9
) and (
3.16
) we have

\check{\mathscr{L}}_{\lambda}(x)=\mathfrak{E}_{\lambda}\left(\Phi_{\bar{\gamma}}(x)\right)\quad\forall x\in B_{{\bf X}^{\bot}}(0,\delta)

(3.31)

because
\delta\leq 2\iota<\rho_{0}
and
x\in B_{{\bf X}^{\bot}}(0,\delta)
imply that

(t,x(t),\dot{x}(t))\in\mathbb{R}\times B^{n}_{2\iota}(0)\times B^{n}_{\rho_{0}}(0)

and so
\check{L}(\lambda,t,x(t),\dot{x}(t))=L^{\star}(\lambda,t,x(t),\dot{x}(t))

for all
t\in\mathbb{R}
.

It follows that each

x_{m}:=(\Phi_{\bar{\gamma}})^{-1}(\beta_{m})\in\{\xi\in{\bf X}^{\bot}\,|\,\|\xi\|_{C^{1}}<\delta_{m}\}

is a critical point of
\check{\mathscr{L}}_{\lambda_{k_{m}}}
in
{\bf H}
(and hence that
of
\check{\mathscr{L}}_{\lambda_{k_{m}}}^{\bot}
in
{\bf H}^{\bot}
)
and they are distinct each other. Moreover
\|x_{m}\|_{1,2}\leq\sqrt{\tau}\|x_{m}\|_{C^{1}}\to 0
.
Hence
(\mu,0)\in\Lambda\times(\mathcal{U}\cap{\bf H}^{\bot})
is a bifurcation point of
\nabla{\check{\mathscr{L}}}_{\lambda}^{\bot}=0

in
\Lambda\times(\mathcal{U}\cap{\bf H}^{\bot})
.

Since
\check{\mathscr{L}}_{\lambda}
is
C^{2-0}
and

\nabla\check{\mathscr{L}}_{\lambda}:\mathcal{U}\to{\bf H}

has the Gâteaux derivative
{B}_{\lambda}(x)\in{\mathscr{L}}_{s}({\bf H})
at
x\in\mathcal{U}
,

{\check{\mathscr{L}}}_{\lambda}^{\bot}
is
C^{2-0}
and
\nabla{\check{\mathscr{L}}}_{\lambda}^{\bot}

has a Gâteaux derivative
\mathbb{B}_{\lambda}(x)\in{\mathscr{L}}_{s}({\bf H}^{\bot})

at
x\in\mathcal{U}\cap{\bf H}^{\bot}

given by (
3.26
). From (
3.27
), (
3.30
)
and Propositions
3.4
,
3.5
it easily follows that
the conditions (i)-(iv) of
[
22
, Theorem 3.1]
(
[
25
, Theorem C.6]
) are satisfied with
\mathcal{F}_{\lambda}={\check{\mathscr{L}}}_{\lambda}^{\bot}
and

H=X={\bf H}^{\bot}
and
U=\mathcal{U}\cap{\bf H}^{\bot}
.
Therefore
m^{0}_{\tau}(\check{\mathscr{L}}_{\mu}^{\bot},0)\geq 1
.
This and (
3.11
), (
3.17
) and (
3.28
) lead to

m^{0}_{\tau}(\mathfrak{E}_{\mu},\bar{\gamma})=m^{0}_{\tau}(\check{\mathscr{L}}_{\mu},0)\geq 2
.
∎

Note that Propositions
3.8
,
3.7
are not used
in the proof of Theorem
1.6
. However, they are necessary
for proofs of Theorems
1.7
,
1.9
.

Proof of Theorem
1.7
.

The original
\Lambda
can be replaced by the compact and sequential compact subset

\hat{\Lambda}=\{\mu,\lambda^{+}_{k},\lambda^{-}_{k}\,|\,k\in\mathbb{N}\}
.
Follow the notations above. By Propositions
3.7
,
3.4
,
3.5
,
and (
3.15
) and (
3.29
),
the conditions of
[
24
, Theorem 3.3]
(or
[
22
, Theorem A.3]
) are satisfied with

\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}=\check{\mathscr{L}}_{\lambda}
,
H={\bf H}
,
X={\bf X}
,
U=\mathcal{U}
and
\lambda^{\ast}=\mu
.
From these, (
3.25
)-(
3.26
)
and (
3.27
), (
3.30
) and
Proposition
3.6
it follows that

\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}=\check{\mathscr{L}}_{\lambda}^{\bot}
,
H={\bf H}^{\bot}
,
X={\bf X}^{\bot}
,
U=\mathcal{U}\cap{\bf H}^{\bot}

and
\lambda^{\ast}=\mu
satisfy the conditions of
[
24
, Theorem 3.3]
(or
[
22
, Theorem A.3]
).

By the assumptions (a)–(b) of Theorem
1.7
we may use
(
3.11
), (
3.17
) and (
3.28
) to deduce
that
m^{0}_{\tau}(\check{\mathscr{L}}_{\mu}^{\bot},0)\geq 1

and that for each
k\in\mathbb{N}
,

[m^{-}_{\tau}(\check{\mathscr{L}}^{\bot}_{\lambda_{k}^{-}},0),m^{-}_{\tau}(\check{\mathscr{L}}^{\bot}_{\lambda_{k}^{-}},0)+m^{0}_{\tau}(\check{\mathscr{L}}^{\bot}_{\lambda_{k}^{-}},0)]\cap[m^{-}_{\tau}(\check{\mathscr{L}}^{\bot}_{\lambda_{k}^{+}},0),m^{-}_{\tau}(\check{\mathscr{L}}^{\bot}_{\lambda_{k}^{+}},0)+m^{0}_{\tau}(\check{\mathscr{L}}^{\bot}_{\lambda_{k}^{+}},0)]=\emptyset

and either
m^{0}_{\tau}(\check{\mathscr{L}}^{\bot}_{\lambda_{k}^{-}},0)=0
or
m^{0}_{\tau}(\check{\mathscr{L}}^{\bot}_{\lambda_{k}^{+}},0)=0
.

Thus from
[
25
, Theorem C.4]
we conclude that there exists an infinite sequence

(\lambda_{k},x_{k})\subset\hat{\Lambda}\times{\bf H}^{\bot}\setminus\{(\mu,0)\}

converging to
(\mu,0)
such that each
x_{k}\neq 0
and satisfies

\check{\mathscr{L}}^{\bot}_{\lambda_{k}}(x_{k})=0
for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
.

Fix
0<\delta\leq 2\iota
. Let
\Omega_{\delta}
be as in Proposition
3.10
.
By Proposition
3.8
, passing to a subsequence (if necessary)
we may assume: each
x_{k}
is
C^{4}
and a critical point of

\check{\mathscr{L}}_{\lambda_{k}}
,
\|x_{k}\|_{C^{2}}<\delta\;\forall k
and
\|x_{k}\|_{C^{2}}\to 0
.
Then each
\gamma_{k}:=\Phi_{\bar{\gamma}}(x_{k})\in\Omega_{\delta}
is
a
C^{6}
solution of the corresponding problem (
1.4
) with
\lambda=\lambda_{k}
,
k=1,2,\cdots
,
and
(\gamma_{k})
converges to
\bar{\gamma}
on any compact interval
I\subset{\mathbb{R}}
in
C^{2}
-topology as
k\to\infty
.

Since
d\Phi_{{\bar{\gamma}}}(0)[\zeta_{0}]=\dot{\bar{\gamma}}
and

T_{\bar{\gamma}}\Omega_{\delta}=d\Phi_{\bar{\gamma}}(0)({\bf X}^{\bot})
,

\mathbb{R}\zeta_{0}+{\bf X}^{\bot}={\bf X}
implies
\mathbb{R}\dot{\bar{\gamma}}+T_{\bar{\gamma}}\Omega_{\delta}=T_{\bar{\gamma}}\mathcal{X}^{1}_{\tau}(M,\mathbb{I}_{g})
, that is,
the
C^{4}
embedded circle
\mathcal{O}=\mathbb{R}\cdot\bar{\gamma}
(
because of periodicity of

\bar{\gamma}
) and
\Omega_{\delta}
are transversely intersecting at
\bar{\gamma}
.
It follows that there exists a neighborhood
\mathscr{V}
of
\bar{\gamma}
in
\mathcal{X}^{1}_{\tau}(M,\mathbb{I}_{g})
such that

\mathscr{V}\cap\mathcal{O}\cap\Omega_{\delta}=\{\bar{\gamma}\}
.
Because
\|x_{k}\|_{C^{2}}\to 0
, there exists
k_{0}>0
such that for each
k>k_{0}
,

\gamma_{k}=\Phi_{\bar{\gamma}}(x_{k})\in\mathscr{V}\cap\Omega_{\delta}\setminus\{\bar{\gamma}\}
and
the
C^{4}
immersed submanifold
\mathbb{R}\cdot\gamma_{k}

transversely intersect with
\Omega_{\delta}
at
\gamma_{k}
. Hence

\mathbb{R}\cdot\gamma_{k}\neq\mathcal{O}\quad\hbox{for any $k>k_{0}$.}

(3.32)

(Otherwise,
\mathcal{O}
and
\Omega_{\delta}
have
at least two distinct intersecting points
\gamma_{k}
and
\bar{\gamma}
in
\mathscr{V}
.)

We conclude that
\{\mathbb{R}\cdot\gamma_{k}\,|\,k\in\mathbb{N}\}
is an infinite set.
(Thus
(\gamma_{k})
has a subsequence which only consists of
{\mathbb{R}}
-distinct elements.
The proof is completed.)
Otherwise, passing to a subsequence we may assume that all
\gamma_{k}

are
{\mathbb{R}}
-same, i.e.,
\gamma_{k}=s_{k}\cdot\gamma^{\ast}
for some
s_{k}\in\mathbb{R}
,
where
\gamma^{\ast}:\mathbb{R}\to M
satisfies (
1.4
) with
\lambda=\lambda_{k}
,
k=1,2,\cdots
.
Since all partial derivatives of
L(\lambda,\cdot)
of order no more than two depend continuously on

(\lambda,x,v)\in\Lambda\times TM
, it easily follows that
\gamma^{\ast}
satisfies (
1.4
) with
\lambda=\mu
.
Clearly,
\bar{\gamma}
sits in the intersection of
\mathcal{O}
and the closure of
\mathbb{R}\cdot\gamma^{\ast}
. By the assumption
(c) of Theorem
1.7

\mathbb{R}\cdot\gamma^{\ast}
is closed and so equal to
\mathcal{O}
,
namely
\mathbb{R}\cdot\gamma_{k}=\mathcal{O}\;\forall k
,
which contradicts (
3.32
).
∎

In order to prove Theorem
1.9

we also need some preparations. Consider the
C^{4}
Hilbert–Riemannian manifold

\Lambda_{\tau}(M,\mathbb{I}_{g})=\{\gamma\in W^{1,2}_{loc}({\mathbb{R}},M)\,|\,\gamma(t+\tau)=\mathbb{I}_{g}(\gamma(t))\;\forall t\}

with the natural Riemannian metric given by (
1.2
); see
[
36
, Theorem 4.2]
(or
[
34
, Theorem(8)]
).
Let
\|\cdot\|_{1}=\sqrt{\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{1}}
be the induced norm.

Since
{\mathbb{R}}_{\bar{\gamma}}
is an infinite cyclic subgroup of
{\mathbb{R}}
with generator
p>0
,
i.e.,
\bar{\gamma}
has the least period
p
,
the orbit
{\cal O}:={\mathbb{R}}\cdot\bar{\gamma}
is an
{\mathbb{R}}
-invariant
compact connected
C^{3}
submanifold of
\Lambda_{\tau}(M,\mathbb{I}_{g})
,
precisely an
C^{3}
embedded circle
S^{1}(p):={\mathbb{R}}/p{\mathbb{Z}}
.
Let
\pi:N{\cal O}\to{\cal O}
be the normal bundle
of
{\cal O}
in
\Lambda_{\tau}(M,\mathbb{I}_{g})
. It is a
C^{2}

Hilbert vector bundle over
{\cal O}
(because
T{\cal O}
is a
C^{2}
subbundle of

T_{\cal O}\Lambda_{\tau}(M,\mathbb{I}_{g})
), and

XN{\cal O}:=T_{\cal O}\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(M,\mathbb{I}_{g})\cap N{\cal O}

is a
C^{2}
Banach vector subbundle
of
T_{\cal O}\mathcal{X}_{\tau}(M,\mathbb{I}_{g})
by
[
20
, Proposition 5.1]
.
Recall that
3\iota
is less than the injectivity
radius of
g
at each point on
\bar{\gamma}(\mathbb{R})
.
For
0<\nu\leq 3\iota
we define

N{\cal O}(\nu):=\{(\gamma,v)\in N{\cal O}\,|\,\|v\|_{1,2}<\nu\}\quad\hbox{and}\quad XN{\cal O}(\nu):=\{(\gamma,v)\in XN{\cal O}\,|\,\|v\|_{C^{1}}<\nu\}.

Clearly,
XN{\cal O}(\nu)\subset N{\cal O}(\sqrt{\tau}\nu)
and
there exist natural induced
{\mathbb{R}}
-actions on these bundles given by

(\gamma,v)\mapsto(s\cdot\gamma,s\cdot v)\quad\forall s\in\mathbb{R}.

Using the exponential map
\exp
of
g
we define the map

{\rm EXP}:T\Lambda_{\tau}(M,\mathbb{I}_{g})(\sqrt{\tau}\nu)=\{(\gamma,v)\in T\Lambda_{\tau}(M,\mathbb{I}_{g})\,|\,\|v\|_{1,2}<\sqrt{\tau}\nu\}\to\Lambda_{\tau}(M,\mathbb{I}_{g})

(3.33)

by
{\rm EXP}(\gamma,v)(t)=\exp_{\gamma(t)}v(t)\;\forall t\in{\mathbb{R}}
. Clearly,
{\rm EXP}
is equivariant, i.e.,

s\cdot({\rm EXP}(\gamma,v))={\rm EXP}(s\cdot\gamma,s\cdot v)\quad\forall s\in\mathbb{R}.

It follows from
[
20
, Lemma 5.2]
that
{\rm EXP}
is
C^{2}
.
For sufficiently small
\nu>0
,
{\rm EXP}
gives rise to a
C^{2}

diffeomorphism
\digamma:N{\cal O}(\sqrt{\tau}\nu)\to{\cal N}({\cal O},\sqrt{\tau}\nu)
,
where
{\cal N}({\cal O},\sqrt{\tau}\nu)
is an open
neighborhood of
{\cal O}
in
\Lambda_{\tau}(M,\mathbb{I}_{g})
.
Let
{\cal X}({\cal O},\nu):=\digamma(XN{\cal O}(\nu))
, which is contained in
{\cal N}({\cal O},\sqrt{\tau}\nu)
.

Lemma 3.11
.

Suppose that
(\mathbb{I}_{g})^{l}=id_{M}
for some
l\in\mathbb{N}
, and that

0<\delta\leq 2\iota
is so small that

\Omega_{\delta}=\Phi_{\bar{\gamma}}\left(B_{{\bf X}^{\bot}}(0,\delta)\right)\subset{\cal X}({\cal O},\nu).

Then for any two different points
\gamma_{i}\in\Omega_{\delta}
,
i=1,2
,
either they are
\mathbb{R}
-distinct,
or there exists an integer
0<m\leq l
such that
\gamma_{2}=(m\tau)\cdot\gamma_{2}
or
\gamma_{1}=(m\tau)\cdot\gamma_{1}
.
In particular, if
l=1
and
\tau
is equal to the minimal period
p
of
\bar{\gamma}
,
then any two different points in
\Omega_{\delta}
are
\mathbb{R}
-distinct.

Proof.

Let different points
\xi_{1},\xi_{2}\in B_{{\bf X}^{\bot}}(0,\delta)
be such that

\gamma_{1}=\Phi_{\bar{\gamma}}(\xi_{1})
and
\gamma_{2}=\Phi_{\bar{\gamma}}(\xi_{2})
are
\mathbb{R}
-same.
Then we have
s\geq 0
such that
s\cdot\gamma_{1}=\gamma_{2}
.
Since
\mathbb{I}^{l}_{g}=id_{M}
implies
(kl\tau)\cdot\gamma_{1}=\gamma_{1}
and
(s-kl\tau)\cdot\gamma_{1}=s\cdot\gamma_{1}
for any
k\in\mathbb{Z}

we can assume
0\leq s<l\tau
. By (
3.6
),

(\bar{\gamma},\sum^{n}_{i=1}\xi_{2}^{i}e_{i})
and

(s\cdot\bar{\gamma},\sum^{n}_{i=1}(s\cdot\xi_{1}^{i})(s\cdot e_{i}))

belong to
N{\cal O}(\sqrt{\tau}\nu)
.
Note that

\displaystyle(s\cdot\gamma_{1})(t)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\gamma_{1}(s+t)=\exp_{{\bar{\gamma}}(s+t)}\bigg(\sum^{n}_{i=1}\xi_{1}^{i}(s+t)e_{i}(s+t)\bigg)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\rm EXP}\bigg(s\cdot\bar{\gamma},\sum^{n}_{i=1}(s\cdot\xi_{1}^{i})(s\cdot e_{i})\bigg)(t)

and so

s\cdot\gamma_{1}=\digamma\bigg(s\cdot\bar{\gamma},\sum^{n}_{i=1}(s\cdot\xi_{1}^{i})(s\cdot e_{i})\bigg).

Similarity, we have

\gamma_{2}=\digamma\bigg(\bar{\gamma},\sum^{n}_{i=1}\xi_{2}^{i}e_{i}\bigg).

Then
s\cdot\bar{\gamma}=\bar{\gamma}
and

\sum^{n}_{i=1}(s\cdot\xi_{1}^{i})(s\cdot e_{i})=\sum^{n}_{i=1}\xi_{2}^{i}e_{i}.

The former implies
s\in\mathbb{R}_{\bar{\gamma}}\subset\{[0],\cdots,[(l-1)\tau]\}
,
where
[q\tau]=q\tau+l\mathbb{Z}
.
Hence
s\in\{0,\cdots,l-1\}
. Combining with the latter we obtain
\xi_{1}=\xi_{2}
,
and therefore a contradiction.

When
l=1
and
\tau
is equal to the minimal period
p
of

\bar{\gamma}
,
\mathbb{R}_{\bar{\gamma}}=\{0\}
and so

\xi_{1}=\xi_{2}
. A contradiction is obtained.
∎

Proof of Theorem
1.9
.

The original
\Lambda
may be replaced by
\hat{\Lambda}=[\mu-\varepsilon,\mu+\varepsilon]
.
By the first paragraph in the proof of Theorem
1.7

we have checked that

\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}=\check{\mathscr{L}}_{\lambda}^{\bot}
,
H={\bf H}^{\bot}
,
X={\bf X}^{\bot}
,

U=\mathcal{U}\cap{\bf H}^{\bot}
and
\lambda^{\ast}=\mu

satisfy the conditions of
[
24
, Theorem 3.6]

except for the condition (f).

By the assumptions of Theorem
1.9
and
(
3.11
), (
3.17
) and (
3.28
)
we get that
m^{0}_{\tau}(\check{\mathscr{L}}_{\mu}^{\bot},0)\geq 1
and

m^{0}_{\tau}(\check{\mathscr{L}}^{\bot}_{\lambda},0)=0

for each
\lambda\in\hat{\Lambda}\setminus\{\mu\}
near
\mu
, and that

m^{-}_{\tau}(\mathscr{L}^{\bot}_{\lambda},0)
takes, respectively, values
m^{0}_{\tau}(\check{\mathscr{L}}^{\bot}_{\mu},0)

and
m^{-}_{\tau}(\check{\mathscr{L}}^{\bot}_{\mu},0)+m^{0}_{\tau}(\check{\mathscr{L}}^{\bot}_{\mu},0)-1

as
\lambda\in\hat{\Lambda}
varies in two deleted half neighborhoods of
\mu
.
These mean that the condition (f) of
[
25
, Theorem C.7]
(
[
24
, Theorem 3.6]
) is satisfied.
Therefore one of the following alternatives occurs:

(i)

There exists a sequence
(x_{k})\subset{\bf H}^{\bot}\setminus\{0\}
converging to
0
in
{\bf H}^{\bot}

such that
\nabla\check{\mathscr{L}}^{\bot}_{\mu}(x_{k})=0
for all
k
.

(ii)

For each
\lambda\in\Lambda\setminus\{\mu\}
near
\mu
,

\nabla\check{\mathscr{L}}^{\bot}_{\lambda}(w)=0
has a solution
x_{\lambda}\in{\bf X}^{\bot}
different from
0
,
which converges to
0
in
{\bf X}^{\bot}
as
\lambda\to\mu
.

(iii)

Given a neighborhood
\mathfrak{W}
of
0
in
{\bf X}^{\bot}
,
there is a one-sided neighborhood
\Lambda^{0}
of
\mu
such that
for any
\lambda\in\Lambda^{0}\setminus\{\mu\}
,
\nabla\check{\mathscr{L}}^{\bot}_{\lambda}(w)=0

has at least two nonzero solutions in
\mathfrak{W}
,
x_{\lambda}^{1}
and
x_{\lambda}^{2}
,
which can also be required to satisfy
\check{\mathscr{L}}^{\bot}_{\lambda}(x_{\lambda}^{1})\neq\check{\mathscr{L}}^{\bot}_{\lambda}(x^{2}_{\lambda})

provided that
m^{0}_{\tau}(\check{\mathscr{L}}_{\mu}^{\bot},0)\geq 2

and
\nabla\check{\mathscr{L}}^{\bot}_{\lambda}(w)=0
has only
finitely many nonzero solutions in
\mathfrak{W}
.

Let
\delta>0
satisfy Proposition
3.10
and Lemma

.
By Proposition
3.8
,
we obtain:

\bullet

In case (i), passing to a subsequence (if necessary) all

and

Therefore each
\gamma_{k}:=\Phi_{\bar{\gamma}}(x_{k})\in\Omega_{\delta}

a

1.4
) with
\lambda=\mu
,
and
(\gamma_{k})

\bar{\gamma}
on any compact interval

.

In case (ii), when

C^{4}

and

as
\lambda\to\mu
.

\gamma_{\lambda}:=\Phi_{\bar{\gamma}}(x_{\lambda})\in\Omega_{\delta}\setminus\{\bar{\gamma}\}

C^{6}

),

\gamma_{\lambda}

.

\bullet

In case (iii), we can require that the neighborhood
\mathfrak{W}

and
\mathfrak{W}\subset B_{{\bf X}^{\bot}}(0,\delta)
.
The latter implies
\Phi_{\bar{\gamma}}(\mathfrak{W})\subset\Omega_{\delta}
.

and

and critical points of

,
and satisfy:

and

,

. Consequently,

\gamma_{\lambda}^{1}:=\Phi_{\bar{\gamma}}(x_{\lambda}^{1})
and

belong to

solutions of the corresponding problem (
1.4
).
When
m^{0}_{\tau}(\mathfrak{E}_{\mu},\bar{\gamma})=m^{0}_{\tau}(\check{\mathscr{L}}_{\mu}^{\bot},0)+1\geq 3
,
and (
1.4
) with parameter value
\lambda
has

\mathbb{R}
-distinct solutions in
\mathcal{W}
which are
\mathbb{R}
-distinct from
\bar{\gamma}
,
it is clear that
\nabla\check{\mathscr{L}}^{\bot}_{\lambda}(w)=0
has only finitely many nonzero solutions in
\mathfrak{W}
,
and therefore we can require that
x_{\lambda}^{1}
and
x_{\lambda}^{2}
satisfy
\check{\mathscr{L}}^{\bot}_{\lambda}(x_{\lambda}^{1})\neq\check{\mathscr{L}}^{\bot}_{\lambda}(x^{2}_{\lambda})
,
which implies
\mathfrak{E}_{\lambda}(\gamma^{1}_{\lambda})\neq\mathfrak{E}_{\lambda}(\gamma^{2}_{\lambda})
,

Proof of Theorem
1.8
.

The original
\Lambda
may be replaced by
\hat{\Lambda}=\alpha([0,1])
.
By the first paragraph in the proof of Theorem
1.7

we have checked that
\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}=\check{\mathscr{L}}_{\lambda}^{\bot}
,
H={\bf H}^{\bot}
,

X={\bf X}^{\bot}
,

U=\mathcal{U}\cap{\bf H}^{\bot}
satisfy the assumptions a)-c) and (i)-(v) of
[
25
, Theorem C.5]

for any
\lambda^{\ast}\in\hat{\Lambda}
. The condition (d) of Theorem
1.8

can be translated into:

[m^{-}_{\tau}(\check{\mathscr{L}}^{\bot}_{\lambda^{-}},0),m^{-}_{\tau}(\check{\mathscr{L}}^{\bot}_{\lambda^{-}},0)+m^{0}_{\tau}(\check{\mathscr{L}}^{\bot}_{\lambda^{-}},0)]\cap[m^{-}_{\tau}(\check{\mathscr{L}}^{\bot}_{\lambda^{+}},0),m^{-}_{\tau}(\check{\mathscr{L}}^{\bot}_{\lambda^{+}},0)+m^{0}_{\tau}(\check{\mathscr{L}}^{\bot}_{\lambda^{+}},0)]=\emptyset

and either
m^{0}_{\tau}(\check{\mathscr{L}}^{\bot}_{\lambda^{-}},0)=0
or
m^{0}_{\tau}(\check{\mathscr{L}}^{\bot}_{\lambda^{+}},0)=0
.

Hence the condition (e.3) of
[
25
, Theorem C.5]
is satisfied.
Thus
[
25
, Theorem C.4]

concludes that there exists
\mu\in\alpha([0,1])
and an infinite sequence
(\lambda_{k},x_{k})\subset\hat{\Lambda}\times{\bf H}^{\bot}\setminus\{(\mu,0)\}

converging to
(\mu,0)
such that each
x_{k}\neq 0
and satisfies

\check{\mathscr{L}}^{\bot}_{\lambda_{k}}(x_{k})=0
for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
.
Moreover,
\mu
is not equal to
\lambda^{+}
(resp.
\lambda^{-}
) if

m^{0}_{\tau}(\check{\mathscr{L}}^{\bot}_{\lambda^{+}},0)=0

(resp.
m^{0}_{\tau}(\check{\mathscr{L}}^{\bot}_{\lambda^{-}},0)=0
).
We can assume
\lambda_{k}=\alpha(t_{k})
for some
(t_{k})\subset[0,1]

converging to
\bar{t}\in[0,1]
.

Fix
0<\delta\leq 2\iota
. Let
\Omega_{\delta}
satisfy
Proposition
3.10
and Lemma
3.11
.
By Proposition
3.8
, passing to a subsequence (if necessary)
we may assume: each
x_{k}
is
C^{4}
and a critical point of

\check{\mathscr{L}}_{\lambda_{k}}
,
\|x_{k}\|_{C^{2}}<\delta\;\forall k
and
\|x_{k}\|_{C^{2}}\to 0
.
Then each
\gamma_{k}:=\Phi_{\bar{\gamma}}(x_{k})\in\Omega_{\delta}
is
a
C^{6}
solution of the corresponding problem (
1.4
) with
\lambda=\lambda_{k}
,
k=1,2,\cdots
,
and
(\gamma_{k})
converges to
\bar{\gamma}
on any compact interval
I\subset{\mathbb{R}}
in
C^{2}
-topology as
k\to\infty
.
We can assume that all
x_{k}
are distinct each other.
By Lemma
3.11
each
\gamma_{k}
has at most
l

\mathbb{R}
-same points in
\{\gamma_{k}\,|\,k\in\mathbb{N}\}
.
Hence
(\gamma_{k})
has a subsequence
(\gamma_{k_{i}})
consisting of
completely
\mathbb{R}
-distinct points.
The required assertions are proved.
∎

3.4
Proofs of Propositions
3.7
,
3.8

Proof of Proposition
3.7
.

Step 1
(
Prove that
\hat{\Lambda}\times\mathcal{U}\ni(\lambda,x)\mapsto\check{\mathscr{L}}_{\lambda}(x)\in{\mathbb{R}}
is continuous
).
Indeed, for any two points
(\lambda,x)
and
(\lambda_{0},x_{0})

in
\hat{\Lambda}\times\mathcal{U}
we can write

\displaystyle\check{\mathscr{L}}_{\lambda}(x)-\check{\mathscr{L}}_{\lambda_{0}}(x_{0})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[\int^{\tau}_{0}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,x(t),\dot{x}(t))dt-\int^{\tau}_{0}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,x_{0}(t),\dot{x}_{0}(t))dt\right]

\displaystyle+\left[\int^{\tau}_{0}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,x_{0}(t),\dot{x}_{0}(t))dt-\int^{\tau}_{0}\check{L}_{\lambda_{0}}(t,x_{0}(t),\dot{x}_{0}(t))dt\right].

As
(\lambda,x)\to(\lambda_{0},x_{0})
, we derive from (L6) in Lemma
3.1
and
[
23
, Proposition C.1]
(resp.
(L6) in Lemma
3.1
and the Lebesgue dominated convergence theorem) that
the first (resp. second) bracket on the right side converges to the zero.

(
Actually
, we only need that
\hat{\Lambda}\times\mathcal{U}^{X}\ni(\lambda,x)\mapsto\check{\mathscr{L}}_{\lambda}(x)\in{\mathbb{R}}
is continuous. This can easily be proved as follows.
For any fixed point
x_{0}\in\mathcal{U}^{X}
, we can take a positive
\rho>0
such that
\rho>\sup_{t}|\dot{x}_{0}(t)|
.
Since
E_{\bar{\gamma}}
is an orthogonal matrix, and
\check{L}:\hat{\Lambda}\times\mathbb{R}\times\bar{B}^{n}_{2\iota}(0)\times\mathbb{R}^{n}\to{\mathbb{R}}
is continuous,
we deduce that
\check{L}
is uniformly continuous in
\hat{\Lambda}\times\mathbb{R}\times\bar{B}^{n}_{2\iota}(0)\times\bar{B}^{n}_{\rho}(0)
.
This and (
3.12
) lead to the desired claim.)

Step 2
(
Prove that
\hat{\Lambda}\times\mathcal{U}^{X}\ni(\lambda,x)\mapsto A_{\lambda}(x)\in{\bf X}
is continuous
).
For
(\lambda_{1},x),(\lambda_{2},y)\in\hat{\Lambda}\times\mathcal{U}^{X}
, and
\xi\in{\bf H}
, since

\displaystyle d\check{\mathscr{L}}_{\lambda_{1}}(x)[\xi]-d\check{\mathscr{L}}_{\lambda_{2}}(y)[\xi]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\tau}\left(\partial_{q}\check{L}(\lambda_{1},t,x(t),\dot{x}(t))-\partial_{q}\check{L}(\lambda_{2},t,y(t),\dot{y}(t))\right)\cdot\xi(t)dt

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\hskip-17.07164pt\int_{0}^{\tau}\left(\partial_{v}\check{L}\left(\lambda_{1},t,x(t),\dot{x}(t))-\partial_{v}\check{L}(\lambda_{2},t,y(t),\dot{y}(t))\right)\cdot\dot{\xi}(t)\right)\,dt,

we have

\displaystyle\|\nabla\check{\mathscr{L}}_{\lambda_{1}}(x)-\nabla\check{\mathscr{L}}_{\lambda_{2}}(y)\|_{1,2}\hskip-5.69054pt

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\hskip-5.69054pt\left(\int_{0}^{\tau}\left|\partial_{q}\check{L}(\lambda_{1},t,x(t),\dot{x}(t))-\partial_{q}\check{L}(\lambda_{2},t,y(t),\dot{y}(t))\right|^{2}dt\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\hskip-17.07164pt\left(\int_{0}^{\tau}\left|\partial_{v}\check{L}(\lambda_{1},t,x(t),\dot{x}(t))-\partial_{v}\check{L}(\lambda_{2},t,y(t),\dot{y}(t))\right|^{2}dt\right)^{1/2}.

Fix a point
(\lambda_{1},x)\in\hat{\Lambda}\times\mathcal{U}^{X}
.
Then
\{(\lambda_{1},t,x(t),\dot{x}(t))\,|\,t\in[0,\tau]\}

is a compact subset of
\hat{\Lambda}\times[0,\tau]\times B^{n}_{2\iota}(0)\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
.
Since
\partial_{q}\check{L}
and
\partial_{v}\check{L}
are uniformly
continuous in any compact neighborhood of this compact subset we deduce that

\|\nabla\check{\mathscr{L}}_{\lambda_{1}}(x)-\nabla\check{\mathscr{L}}_{\lambda_{2}}(y)\|_{C^{0}}\leq C_{\tau}\|\nabla\check{\mathscr{L}}_{\lambda_{1}}(x)-\nabla\check{\mathscr{L}}_{\lambda_{2}}(y)\|_{1,2}\to 0

(3.34)

provided
(\lambda_{2},y)\in\hat{\Lambda}\times\mathcal{U}^{X}
converges to
(\lambda_{1},x)
in
\hat{\Lambda}\times\mathcal{U}^{X}
.

By (
3.19
) and (
3.24
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\nabla\check{\mathscr{L}}_{\lambda}(x)(t)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{e^{t}}{2}\int^{\infty}_{t}e^{-s}\left(\partial_{q}\check{L}_{{\lambda}}\bigl(s,x(s),\dot{x}(s)\bigr)-\mathfrak{R}_{\lambda}^{x}(s)\right)\,ds

(3.35)

\displaystyle-\frac{e^{-t}}{2}\int^{t}_{-\infty}e^{s}\left(\partial_{q}\check{L}_{{\lambda}}\bigl(s,x(s),\dot{x}(s)\bigr)-\mathfrak{R}_{\lambda}^{x})(s)\right)\,ds

\displaystyle+\partial_{v}\check{L}_{{\lambda}}\bigl(t,x(t),\dot{x}(t)\bigr)+\mathfrak{M}\int^{\tau}_{0}\partial_{v}\check{L}_{{\lambda}}(s,x(s),\dot{x}(s))ds,

where
\mathfrak{R}_{\lambda}^{x}
is given by (
3.2
).
Let
\mathfrak{T}_{\lambda}^{x}(t)
denote a column vector

\displaystyle\left[\left(\oplus_{l\leq p}\frac{\sin\theta_{l}}{2-2\cos\theta_{l}}{\scriptscriptstyle\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&-1\\
1&0\end{array}\right)}-\frac{1}{2}I_{2p}\right)\oplus{\rm diag}(a_{p+1}(t),\cdots,a_{\sigma}(t))\right]\int^{\tau}_{0}\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda}\bigl(s,x(s),\dot{x}(s)\bigr)ds.

Then we have a constant
C(E_{\bar{\gamma}})>0
only depending on

E_{\bar{\gamma}}
such that for all
t
,

\big|\mathfrak{T}_{\lambda_{1}}^{x}(t)-\mathfrak{T}_{\lambda_{2}}^{y}(t)\big|\leq C(E_{\gamma_{0}})(1+|t|)\int^{\tau}_{0}\Big|\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda_{1}}\bigl(s,x(s),\dot{x}(s)\bigr)-\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda_{2}}\bigl(s,y(s),\dot{y}(s)\bigr)\Big|ds.

(3.37)

By (
3.2
) and (
3.24
) we observe

\displaystyle\mathfrak{R}_{\lambda}^{x}(t)=\int^{t}_{0}\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda}\bigl(s,x(s),\dot{x}(s)\bigr)ds+\mathfrak{T}_{\lambda}^{x}(t),

(3.38)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\mathfrak{T}_{\lambda}^{x}(t)=\mathfrak{M}\int^{\tau}_{0}\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda}\bigl(s,x(s),\dot{x}(s)\bigr)ds.

(3.39)

For
0\leq t\leq\tau
, let

\displaystyle\Gamma^{+}_{\lambda}(x)(t):

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{e^{t}}{2}\int^{\infty}_{t}e^{-s}\left(\partial_{q}\check{L}_{{\lambda}}\bigl(s,x(s),\dot{x}(s)\bigr)-\mathfrak{R}_{\lambda}^{x}(s)\right)ds,

(3.40)

\displaystyle\Gamma^{-}_{\lambda}(x)(t):

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{e^{-t}}{2}\int^{t}_{-\infty}e^{s}\left(\partial_{q}\check{L}_{{\lambda}}\bigl(s,x(s),\dot{x}(s)\bigr)-\mathfrak{R}_{\lambda}^{x}(s)\right)ds.

(3.41)

It follows from (
3.35
) that

\displaystyle\left|\frac{d}{dt}\nabla\check{\mathscr{L}}_{\lambda_{1}}(x)(t)-\frac{d}{dt}\nabla\check{\mathscr{L}}_{\lambda_{2}}(y)(t)\right|

(3.42)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle|\Gamma^{+}_{\lambda_{1}}(x)(t)-\Gamma^{+}_{\lambda_{2}}(y)(t)|+|\Gamma^{-}_{\lambda_{1}}(x)(t)-\Gamma^{-}_{\lambda_{2}}(y)(t)|

\displaystyle+|\partial_{v}\check{L}_{{\lambda_{1}}}\bigl(t,x(t),\dot{x}(t)\bigr)-\partial_{v}\check{L}_{{\lambda_{2}}}\bigl(t,y(t),\dot{y}(t)\bigr)|

\displaystyle+|\mathfrak{M}|\int^{\tau}_{0}|\partial_{v}\check{L}_{{\lambda_{1}}}(s,x(s),\dot{x}(s))-\partial_{v}\check{L}_{{\lambda_{2}}}(s,y(s),\dot{y}(s))|ds.

Let us estimate terms in the right side.

Suppose
k\tau\leq t\leq(k+1)\tau
for some integer
k\geq 0
.
By
\check{L}(\lambda,t+\tau,x,v)=\check{L}(\lambda,t,E_{{\bar{\gamma}}}x,E_{{\bar{\gamma}}}v)

and (
3.37
) we deduce

\displaystyle|\mathfrak{R}_{\lambda_{1}}^{x}(t)-\mathfrak{R}_{\lambda_{2}}^{y}(t)|

(3.43)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int^{(k+1)\tau}_{0}\left|\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda_{1}}\bigl(s,x(s),\dot{x}(s)\bigr)-\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda_{2}}\bigl(s,y(s),\dot{y}(s)\bigr)\right|ds+\big|\mathfrak{T}_{\lambda_{1}}^{x}(t)-\mathfrak{T}_{\lambda_{2}}^{y}(t)\big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(k+1)\int^{\tau}_{0}\left|\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda_{1}}\bigl(s,x(s),\dot{x}(s)\bigr)-\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda_{2}}\bigl(s,y(s),\dot{y}(s)\bigr)\right|ds

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle C(E_{\bar{\gamma}})(1+(k+1)\tau)\int^{\tau}_{0}\Big|\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda_{1}}\bigl(s,x(s),\dot{x}(s)\bigr)-\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda_{2}}\bigl(s,y(s),\dot{y}(s)\bigr)\Big|ds.

Similarly, if
(-k-1)\tau\leq t\leq-k\tau
for some integer
k\geq 0
, we have also

\displaystyle|\mathfrak{R}_{\lambda_{1}}^{x}(t)-\mathfrak{R}_{\lambda_{2}}^{y}(t)|

(3.44)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{(-k-1)\tau}^{0}\left|\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda_{1}}\bigl(s,x(s),\dot{x}(s)\bigr)-\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda_{2}}\bigl(s,y(s),\dot{y}(s)\bigr)\right|ds+\big|\mathfrak{T}_{\lambda_{1}}^{x}(t)-\mathfrak{T}_{\lambda_{2}}^{y}(t)\big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(k+1)\int^{\tau}_{0}\left|\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda_{1}}\bigl(s,x(s),\dot{x}(s)\bigr)-\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda_{2}}\bigl(s,y(s),\dot{y}(s)\bigr)\right|ds

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle C(E_{\bar{\gamma}})(1+(k+1)\tau)\int^{\tau}_{0}\Big|\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda_{1}}\bigl(s,x(s),\dot{x}(s)\bigr)-\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda_{2}}\bigl(s,y(s),\dot{y}(s)\bigr)\Big|ds.

For
0\leq t\leq\tau
, it follows from (
3.40
) and (
3.43
) that

\displaystyle|\Gamma^{+}_{\lambda_{1}}(x)(t)-\Gamma^{+}_{\lambda_{2}}(y)(t)|

(3.45)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{e^{\tau}}{2}\sum^{\infty}_{k=0}\int^{(k+1)\tau}_{k\tau}e^{-s}\left|\partial_{q}\check{L}_{{\lambda_{1}}}\bigl(s,x(s),\dot{x}(s)\bigr)-\partial_{q}\check{L}_{{\lambda_{2}}}\bigl(s,y(s),\dot{y}(s)\bigr)\right|ds

\displaystyle+\frac{e^{\tau}}{2}\sum^{\infty}_{k=0}\int^{(k+1)\tau}_{k\tau}e^{-s}\left|\mathfrak{R}_{\lambda_{1}}^{x}(s)-\mathfrak{R}_{\lambda_{2}}^{y}(s)\right|ds

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{e^{\tau}}{2}\sum^{\infty}_{k=0}e^{-k\tau}\int^{\tau}_{0}\left|\partial_{q}\check{L}_{{\lambda_{1}}}\bigl(s,x(s),\dot{x}(s)\bigr)-\partial_{q}\check{L}_{{\lambda_{2}}}\bigl(s,y(s),\dot{y}(s)\bigr)\right|ds

\displaystyle+\frac{\tau e^{\tau}}{2}\sum^{\infty}_{k=0}e^{-k\tau}(k+1)\int^{\tau}_{0}\left|\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda_{1}}\bigl(s,x(s),\dot{x}(s)\bigr)-\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda_{2}}\bigl(s,y(s),\dot{y}(s)\bigr)\right|ds

\displaystyle+C(E_{\bar{\gamma}})\frac{\tau e^{\tau}}{2}\sum^{\infty}_{k=0}e^{-k\tau}(1+(k+1)\tau)\int^{\tau}_{0}\Big|\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda_{1}}\bigl(s,x(s),\dot{x}(s)\bigr)-\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda_{2}}\bigl(s,y(s),\dot{y}(s)\bigr)\Big|ds.

Similarly, for
0\leq t\leq\tau
, (
3.41
) and (
3.44
) lead to

\displaystyle|\Gamma^{-}_{\lambda_{1}}(x)(t)-\Gamma^{-}_{\lambda_{2}}(y)(t)|

(3.46)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\sum^{\infty}_{k=0}\int^{(-k+1)\tau}_{-k\tau}e^{s}\left|\partial_{q}\check{L}_{{\lambda_{1}}}\bigl(s,x(s),\dot{x}(s)\bigr)-\partial_{q}\check{L}_{{\lambda_{2}}}\bigl(s,y(s),\dot{y}(s)\bigr)\right|ds

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\sum^{\infty}_{k=0}\int^{(-k+1)\tau}_{-k\tau}e^{s}\left|\mathfrak{R}_{\lambda_{1}}^{x}(s)-\mathfrak{R}_{\lambda_{2}}^{y}(s)\right|ds

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{e^{\tau}}{2}\sum^{\infty}_{k=0}e^{-k\tau}\int^{\tau}_{0}\left|\partial_{q}\check{L}_{{\lambda_{1}}}\bigl(s,x(s),\dot{x}(s)\bigr)-\partial_{q}\check{L}_{{\lambda_{2}}}\bigl(s,y(s),\dot{y}(s)\bigr)\right|ds

\displaystyle+\frac{\tau e^{\tau}}{2}\sum^{\infty}_{k=0}e^{-k\tau}(k+1)\int^{\tau}_{0}\left|\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda_{1}}\bigl(s,x(s),\dot{x}(s)\bigr)-\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda_{2}}\bigl(s,y(s),\dot{y}(s)\bigr)\right|ds

\displaystyle+C(E_{\bar{\gamma}})\frac{\tau e^{\tau}}{2}\sum^{\infty}_{k=0}e^{-k\tau}(1+(k+1)\tau)\int^{\tau}_{0}\Big|\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda_{1}}\bigl(s,x(s),\dot{x}(s)\bigr)-\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda_{2}}\bigl(s,y(s),\dot{y}(s)\bigr)\Big|ds.

From these and (
3.42
) we get

\displaystyle\left|\frac{d}{dt}\nabla\check{\mathscr{L}}_{\lambda_{1}}(x)(t)-\frac{d}{dt}\nabla\check{\mathscr{L}}_{\lambda_{2}}(y)(t)\right|

(3.47)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle|\partial_{v}\check{L}_{{\lambda_{1}}}\bigl(t,x(t),\dot{x}(t)\bigr)-\partial_{v}\check{L}_{{\lambda_{2}}}\bigl(t,y(t),\dot{y}(t)\bigr)|

\displaystyle+(C(E_{\bar{\gamma}})C_{\tau}+|\mathfrak{M}|)\int^{\tau}_{0}|\partial_{v}\check{L}_{{\lambda_{1}}}(s,x(s),\dot{x}(s))-\partial_{v}\check{L}_{{\lambda_{2}}}(s,y(s),\dot{y}(s))|ds

\displaystyle+C^{\ast}_{\tau}\int^{\tau}_{0}|\partial_{q}\check{L}_{{\lambda_{1}}}(s,x(s),\dot{x}(s))-\partial_{q}\check{L}_{{\lambda_{2}}}(s,y(s),\dot{y}(s))|ds

for some constant
C^{\ast}_{\tau}>0
and for all
0\leq t\leq\tau
.

As above, for a fixed
(\lambda_{1},x)\in\hat{\Lambda}\times\mathcal{U}^{X}
, by uniform continuity of
\partial_{q}\check{L}
and
\partial_{v}\check{L}

on a compact neighborhood of a compact subset

\{(\lambda_{1},t,x(t),\dot{x}(t))\,|\,t\in[0,\tau]\}
of
\hat{\Lambda}\times[0,\tau]\times B^{n}_{2\iota}(0)\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
,
we can derive from these and (
3.47
) that

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{d}{dt}\nabla\check{\mathscr{L}}_{\lambda_{1}}(x)-\frac{d}{dt}\nabla\check{\mathscr{L}}_{\lambda_{2}}(y)\right\|_{C^{0}}\to 0

provided
(\lambda_{2},y)\in\hat{\Lambda}\times\mathcal{U}^{X}
converges to
(\lambda_{1},x)
in
\hat{\Lambda}\times\mathcal{U}^{X}
.
This and (
3.34
) lead to the second claim.
∎

Proof of Proposition
3.8
.

It is enough to prove sufficiency.
Since
x\in B_{{\bf H}^{\bot}}(0,\varepsilon)
is a critical point
of the restriction of
\check{\mathscr{L}}_{\lambda}
to
B_{{\bf H}^{\bot}}(0,\varepsilon)
,

d\check{\mathscr{L}}_{\lambda}(x)[\xi]=0\;\;\forall\xi\in T_{x}B_{{\bf H}^{\bot}}(0,\varepsilon)={\bf H}^{\bot}.

(3.48)

We shall prove
d\check{\mathscr{L}}_{\lambda}(x)=0
in four steps.

Step 1
(
Prove that
x
is
C^{4}
).
By (
3.48
) we have
\mu(\lambda,x)\in{\mathbb{R}}
such that

\nabla\check{\mathscr{L}}_{\lambda}(x)=\mu(\lambda,x)\zeta_{0}.

(3.49)

That is,
d\check{\mathscr{L}}_{\lambda}(x)[\xi]-\mu(\lambda,x)(\zeta_{0},\xi)_{1,2}=0

for all
\xi\in{\bf H}
.
It follows that

\displaystyle\int^{\tau}_{0}[(\partial_{q}\check{L}_{\lambda}(x(t),\dot{x}(t)),\xi(t))_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}+(\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda}(x(t),\dot{x}(t),\dot{\xi}(t))_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}]dt

\displaystyle-\int^{\tau}_{0}[\mu(\lambda,x)(\zeta_{0}(t),\xi(t))_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}+\mu(\lambda,x)(\dot{\zeta}_{0}(t),\dot{\xi}(t))_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}]dt=0\quad\forall\xi\in{\bf H}.

Define
{\bf L}_{\lambda}(t,q,v)=\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,q,v)-\mu(\lambda,x)(\zeta_{0}(t),q)_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}+\mu(\lambda,x)(\dot{\zeta}_{0}(t),v)_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}
. Then
x(t)
satisfies

\displaystyle\int^{\tau}_{0}[(\partial_{q}{\bf L}_{\lambda}(t,x(t),\dot{x}(t)),\xi(t))_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}+(\partial_{v}{\bf L}_{\lambda}(t,x(t),\dot{x}(t)),\dot{\xi}(t))_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}]dt=0\quad\forall\xi\in{\bf H}.

(3.50)

Since
\zeta_{0}
is
C^{5}
,
{\bf L}_{\lambda}
is
C^{4}
and satisfies the conditions in Lemma
3.1
,
by Remark
3.2

we obtain that
x
is
C^{4}
.

Step 2
(
For any
\epsilon>0
there exists
\delta>0
such that
\|x\|_{1,2}<\delta
implies
|\mu(\lambda,x)|<\epsilon
).
Since
\hat{\Lambda}\subset{\mathbb{R}}
is compact and sequential compact,

\check{L}(\lambda,t+\tau,q,v)=\check{L}(\lambda,t,E_{{\bar{\gamma}}}q,E_{{\bar{\gamma}}}v)
,
and partial derivatives

\partial_{q}\check{L}_{\lambda}(\cdot),\quad\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda}(\cdot),\quad\partial_{qv}\check{L}_{\lambda}(\cdot),\quad\partial_{qq}\check{L}_{\lambda}(\cdot),\quad\partial_{vv}\check{L}_{\lambda}(\cdot),\quad\partial_{vt}\check{L}_{\lambda}(\cdot)

depend continuously on
(\lambda,t,q,v)\in\hat{\Lambda}\times\mathbb{R}\times\bar{B}^{n}_{2\iota}(0)\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
,
by shrinking
\iota>0
(if necessary) it follows from (L3) in Lemma
3.1
that

\displaystyle|\partial_{q}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,q,v)-\partial_{q}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,0,0)|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle|\partial_{q}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,q,v)-\partial_{q}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,q,0)|+|\partial_{q}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,q,0)-\partial_{q}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,0,0)|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq s\leq 1}|\partial_{qv}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,q,sv)|\cdot|v|+\sup_{0\leq s\leq 1}|\partial_{qq}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,sq,0)|\cdot|q|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(|v|+|v|^{2})+C|q|.

Hence we have a constant
C^{\prime}>0
such that

|\partial_{q}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,q,v)|\leq C^{\prime}(1+|v|^{2}),\quad\forall(\lambda,t,q,v)\in\hat{\Lambda}\times\mathbb{R}\times\bar{B}^{n}_{2\iota}(0)\times\mathbb{R}^{n}.

(3.51)

Similarly, we can increase the constant
C^{\prime}>0
so that

|\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,q,v)|\leq C^{\prime}(1+|v|),\quad\forall(\lambda,t,q,v)\in\hat{\Lambda}\times\mathbb{R}\times\bar{B}^{n}_{2\iota}(0)\times\mathbb{R}^{n}.

(3.52)

Since
\nabla\check{\mathscr{L}}_{\lambda}(0)=0
, by (
3.49
) we have

\displaystyle\mu(\lambda,x)(\zeta_{0},\xi)_{1,2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle d\check{\mathscr{L}}_{\lambda}(x)[\xi]-d\check{\mathscr{L}}_{\lambda}(0)[\xi]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int^{\tau}_{0}[(\partial_{q}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,x(t),\dot{x}(t)),\xi(t))_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}-(\partial_{q}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,0,0),\xi(t))_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}]dt

\displaystyle+\int^{\tau}_{0}[(\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,x(t),\dot{x}(t),\dot{\xi}(t))_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}-(\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,0,0),\dot{\xi}(t))_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}]dt.

For the first integral, by the mean value theorem, (L3) in Lemma
3.1
we derive

\displaystyle\Big|\int^{\tau}_{0}[(\partial_{q}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,x(t),\dot{x}(t))-\partial_{q}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,0,0),\xi(t))_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}]dt\Big|

(3.53)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\Big|\int^{\tau}_{0}\int^{1}_{0}[(\partial_{qq}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,sx(t),s\dot{x}(t))x(t),\xi(t))_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}]dtds\Big|

\displaystyle\qquad+\Big|\int^{\tau}_{0}\int^{1}_{0}[(\partial_{qv}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,sx(t),s\dot{x}(t))\dot{x}(t),\xi(t))_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}]dtds\Big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\int^{\tau}_{0}\int^{1}_{0}(1+|s\dot{x}(t))|^{2})|x(t)||\xi(t)|dtds+C\int^{\tau}_{0}\int^{1}_{0}(1+|s\dot{x}(t))|)|\dot{x}(t)||\xi(t)|dtds

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(C_{\tau})^{2}\|x\|_{1,2}\|\xi\|_{1,2}\big(\tau+2\|x\|_{1,2}^{2}\big)+CC_{\tau}\|\xi\|_{1,2}(\sqrt{\tau}\|x\|_{1,2}+\|x\|_{1,2}^{2}).

Here we use inequalities

\|u\|_{C^{0}}\leq(\tau+1)\|u\|_{1,1}\;\forall u\in W^{1,1}([0,\tau];\mathbb{R}^{n})
and

\|u\|_{C^{0}}\leq(\sqrt{\tau}+1/\sqrt{\tau})\|u\|_{1,2}\;\forall u\in W^{1,2}([0,\tau];\mathbb{R}^{n})
.
Similarly, we may estimate the second integral as follows:

\displaystyle\Big|\int^{\tau}_{0}[(\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,x(t),\dot{x}(t),\dot{\xi}(t))_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}-(\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,0,0),\dot{\xi}(t))_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}]dt\Big|

(3.54)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\Big|\int^{\tau}_{0}\int^{1}_{0}[(\partial_{vv}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,sx(t),s\dot{x}(t))\dot{x}(t),\dot{\xi}(t))_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}]dtds\Big|

\displaystyle\qquad+\Big|\int^{\tau}_{0}\int^{1}_{0}[(\partial_{qv}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,sx(t),s\dot{x}(t)){x}(t),\dot{\xi}(t))_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}]dtds\Big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|x\|_{1,2}\|\xi\|_{1,2}+CC_{\tau}\|x\|_{1,2}(\sqrt{\tau}\|\xi\|_{1,2}+\|x\|_{1,2}\|\xi\|_{1,2}).

Hence we get

\displaystyle|\mu(\lambda,x)|\|\zeta_{0}\|_{1,2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(C_{\tau})^{2}\|x\|_{1,2}\big(\tau+2\|x\|_{1,2}^{2}\big)+CC_{\tau}(\sqrt{\tau}\|x\|_{1,2}+\|x\|_{1,2}^{2})

\displaystyle+C\|x\|_{1,2}+CC_{\tau}\|x\|_{1,2}(\sqrt{\tau}+\|x\|_{1,2}).

The desired claim immediately follows because
\zeta_{0}\neq 0
.

Step 3
(
For any
\epsilon>0
there exists

\varepsilon>0
such that
\|x\|_{1,2}\leq\varepsilon
implies
\|x\|_{C^{2}}<\epsilon
).
By (L2) in Lemma
3.1
and the mean value theorem of integrals we deduce

\displaystyle c|v|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int^{1}_{0}\left(\partial_{vv}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,q,sv)[v],v\right)_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}ds=\left(\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,q,v)-\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,q,0),v\right)_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}

and so

c|v|\leq\left|\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,q,v)-\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda}(t,q,0)\right|

(3.55)

for any
(\lambda,t,q,v)\in\hat{\Lambda}\times\mathbb{R}\times\bar{B}^{n}_{2\iota}(0)\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
.
Since we have proved that
x
is
C^{4}
in Step 1, (
3.49
)
also holds in the sense of pointwise, i.e.,

\nabla\check{\mathscr{L}}_{\lambda}(x)(t)=\mu(\lambda,x)\zeta_{0}(t)\;\forall t.

(3.56)

From this and (
3.35
) it follows that

\displaystyle\mu(\lambda,x)\dot{\zeta}_{0}(t)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{e^{t}}{2}\int^{\infty}_{t}e^{-s}\left(\partial_{q}\check{L}_{{\lambda}}\bigl(s,x(s),\dot{x}(s)\bigr)-\mathfrak{R}_{\lambda}^{x}(s)\right)\,ds

(3.57)

\displaystyle-\frac{e^{-t}}{2}\int^{t}_{-\infty}e^{s}\left(\partial_{q}\check{L}_{{\lambda}}\bigl(s,x(s),\dot{x}(s)\bigr)-\mathfrak{R}_{\lambda}^{x})(s)\right)\,ds

\displaystyle+\partial_{v}\check{L}_{{\lambda}}\bigl(t,x(t),\dot{x}(t)\bigr)+\mathfrak{M}\int^{\tau}_{0}\partial_{v}\check{L}_{{\lambda}}(s,x(s),\dot{x}(s))ds,

where
\mathfrak{R}_{\lambda}^{x}
is given by (
3.2
).
In particular, taking
x=0
we get

\displaystyle\mu(\lambda,0)\dot{\zeta}_{0}(t)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{e^{t}}{2}\int^{\infty}_{t}e^{-s}\left(\partial_{q}\check{L}_{{\lambda}}\bigl(s,0,0\bigr)-\mathfrak{R}_{\lambda}^{0}(s)\right)\,ds

(3.58)

\displaystyle-\frac{e^{-t}}{2}\int^{t}_{-\infty}e^{s}\left(\partial_{q}\check{L}_{{\lambda}}\bigl(s,0,0\bigr)-\mathfrak{R}_{\lambda}^{0})(s)\right)\,ds

\displaystyle+\partial_{v}\check{L}_{{\lambda}}\bigl(t,0,0\bigr)+\mathfrak{M}\int^{\tau}_{0}\partial_{v}\check{L}_{{\lambda}}(s,0,0)ds.

(
3.57
) minus (
3.58
) gives rise to

\displaystyle\partial_{v}\check{L}_{{\lambda}}\bigl(t,0,0\bigr)-\partial_{v}\check{L}_{{\lambda}}\bigl(t,x(t),\dot{x}(t)\bigr)

(3.59)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{e^{t}}{2}\int^{\infty}_{t}e^{-s}\left(\partial_{q}\check{L}_{{\lambda}}\bigl(s,x(s),\dot{x}(s)\bigr)-\partial_{q}\check{L}_{{\lambda}}\bigl(s,0,0\bigr)\right)\,ds+\frac{e^{t}}{2}\int^{\infty}_{t}e^{-s}\left(\mathfrak{R}_{\lambda}^{0}(s)-\mathfrak{R}_{\lambda}^{x}(s)\right)\,ds

\displaystyle-\frac{e^{-t}}{2}\int^{t}_{-\infty}e^{s}\left(\partial_{q}\check{L}_{{\lambda}}\bigl(s,x(s),\dot{x}(s)\bigr)-\partial_{q}\check{L}_{{\lambda}}\bigl(s,0,0\bigr)\right)\,ds+\frac{e^{-t}}{2}\int^{t}_{-\infty}e^{s}\left(\mathfrak{R}_{\lambda}^{x}(s)-\mathfrak{R}_{\lambda}^{0}(s)\right)\,ds

\displaystyle+\mathfrak{M}\int^{\tau}_{0}[\partial_{v}\check{L}_{{\lambda}}(s,x(s),\dot{x}(s))-\partial_{v}\check{L}_{{\lambda}}(s,0,0)]ds

\displaystyle+\mu(\lambda,0)\dot{\zeta}_{0}(t)-\mu(\lambda,x)\dot{\zeta}_{0}(t)

For
0\leq t\leq\tau
, it follows from (
3.45
) and (
3.46
) that

\displaystyle\bigg|\frac{e^{t}}{2}\int^{\infty}_{t}e^{-s}\left(\partial_{q}\check{L}_{{\lambda}}\bigl(s,x(s),\dot{x}(s)\bigr)-\partial_{q}\check{L}_{{\lambda}}\bigl(s,0,0\bigr)\right)\,ds+\frac{e^{t}}{2}\int^{\infty}_{t}e^{-s}\left(\mathfrak{R}_{\lambda}^{0}(s)-\mathfrak{R}_{\lambda}^{x}(s)\right)\,ds\bigg|

(3.60)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\biggl[\frac{e^{\tau}}{2}\sum^{\infty}_{k=0}e^{-k\tau}+\frac{\tau e^{\tau}}{2}\sum^{\infty}_{k=0}e^{-k\tau}(k+1)+C(E_{\bar{\gamma}})\frac{\tau e^{\tau}}{2}\sum^{\infty}_{k=0}e^{-k\tau}(1+(k+1)\tau)\biggr]\times

\displaystyle\times\Big|\int^{\tau}_{0}\big[\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda}\bigl(s,x(s),\dot{x}(s)\bigr)-\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda}\bigl(s,0,0\bigr)\big]ds\Big|

and

\displaystyle\bigg|-\frac{e^{-t}}{2}\int^{t}_{-\infty}e^{s}\left(\partial_{q}\check{L}_{{\lambda}}\bigl(s,x(s),\dot{x}(s)\bigr)-\partial_{q}\check{L}_{{\lambda}}\bigl(s,0,0\bigr)\right)\,ds+\frac{e^{-t}}{2}\int^{t}_{-\infty}e^{s}\left(\mathfrak{R}_{\lambda}^{x}(s)-\mathfrak{R}_{\lambda}^{0}(s)\right)\,ds\bigg|

(3.61)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\biggl[\frac{e^{\tau}}{2}\sum^{\infty}_{k=0}e^{-k\tau}+\frac{\tau e^{\tau}}{2}\sum^{\infty}_{k=0}e^{-k\tau}(k+1)+C(E_{\bar{\gamma}})\frac{\tau e^{\tau}}{2}\sum^{\infty}_{k=0}e^{-k\tau}(1+(k+1)\tau)\biggr]\times

\displaystyle\times\Big|\int^{\tau}_{0}\big[\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda}\bigl(s,x(s),\dot{x}(s)\bigr)-\partial_{v}\check{L}_{\lambda}\bigl(s,0,0\bigr)\big]ds\Big|.

An elementary calculation yields

\displaystyle\biggl[\frac{e^{\tau}}{2}\sum^{\infty}_{k=0}e^{-k\tau}+\frac{\tau e^{\tau}}{2}\sum^{\infty}_{k=0}e^{-k\tau}(k+1)+C(E_{\bar{\gamma}})\frac{\tau e^{\tau}}{2}\sum^{\infty}_{k=0}e^{-k\tau}(1+(k+1)\tau)\biggr]

\displaystyle<
